### 46 PSK_SUP
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `PSK_SUP` `embassy/events.rs:103` / linux `BRCMF_E_PSK_SUP` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:70` / whd `WLC_E_PSK_SUP` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:85` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:50` / c-driver `CYW43_EV_PSK_SUP` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:61`. Same spelling apart from the prefix. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask. The `bsscfg:event_msgs` mask sent at init is 24 bytes of 0xFF with only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG and ROAM cleared; this bit stays set `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. Also enabled in the driver-side (software) subscription mask for the duration of a join, with SET_SSID and AUTH, and cleared on drop `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:456-459`<br>c-driver: default mask. 19 mask bytes of 0xff with only bits 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 cleared; this bit stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. A narrower join-time mask naming EV_PSK_SUP=46 exists only inside a `/* */` comment and is not executed; its byte string sets bits 0, 3, 6, 12, 16, 46 and 80, and does not set bit 69 although the comment lists ESCAN_RESULT `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294`<br>linux: enabled. A handler is registered `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:7025`, and `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` sets the bit of every registered handler `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-452`. Until `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` runs (`linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8637`, `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8674`), preinit has only read the firmware's own `event_msgs` mask, set the IF bit and written it back; these sources do not show that firmware default `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`. The mask goes out as the named variable `event_msgs`, or as `event_msgs_ext` through the cyw vendor hook `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:458-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:79-99`<br>whd: enabled while a join handler is registered. `WLC_E_PSK_SUP` is in `join_events[]`, registered at join; the mask is rebuilt from all registered lists `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:158` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2069` `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-419`; an application can enable any code by passing its own list to `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`, which re-sends the mask `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-492` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:230` |
| Handled by | embassy (runner link state `embassy/runner.rs:1273-1292`; join wait `embassy/control.rs:474-496`); linux `brcmf_notify_connect_status` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6780-6842`; whd `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1359-1402`; c-driver `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:403-415` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data. Status, reason and flags are in the common event message header, not in the event data. Header, 48 bytes, no padding (linux `__packed`, whd `#pragma pack(1)`, embassy `repr(C, packed(2))`), multi-byte fields big-endian on the wire (linux declares them `__be16`/`__be32`): version u16 @0, flags u16 @2, event_type u32 @4, status u32 @8, reason u32 @12, auth_type u32 @16, datalen u32 @20, addr[6] @24, ifname[16] @30, ifidx u8 @46, bsscfgidx u8 @47 (offsets computed from the field list; whd and embassy give the 6 and the 16 explicitly, while linux writes `addr[ETH_ALEN]` and `ifname[IFNAMSIZ]` and neither constant is defined in these sources, so the linux size of 48 depends on `ETH_ALEN` = 6 and `IFNAMSIZ` = 16) `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:223-235` `embassy/structs.rs:248-272` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:125` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:133` (the `#pragma pack(1)`) `whd/inc/whd_events.h:160-172` (the struct) `whd/inc/whd_types.h:553` |

- embassy (runner) — link-state tracking. Status 6 with flags 0 and reason 0 sets `key_exchange_ok`, only if `auth_ok` is already set. Otherwise reason 14 is ignored. Any other PSK_SUP clears `key_exchange_ok`. Link is then Up iff `join_ok && (!secure_network || key_exchange_ok)` — reads status (event_type and status truncated to u8, status mapped to `EStatus`), flags, reason — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1229` `embassy/runner.rs:1240-1241` `embassy/runner.rs:1273-1292` `embassy/runner.rs:1296-1304`
- embassy (`wait_for_join`) — the PSK_SUP arms match only when `secure_network` is true; on an open network PSK_SUP falls to `_ => {}`. Secured: raw status 4 with reason 0 is ignored; status 6 ends the join with `Ok`; any other PSK_SUP, including status 4 with a non-zero reason, ends it with `JoinError::AuthenticationFailure` — reads status, reason — `embassy/control.rs:453` `embassy/control.rs:474-496`
- embassy maps the raw status through the generic `EStatus` enum, so 6 appears in its code as `EStatus::UNSOLICITED` and 4 would be `EStatus::ABORT`; its comments say PSK_SUP status codes mean something different `embassy/consts.rs:266` `embassy/consts.rs:270` `embassy/runner.rs:1274` `embassy/control.rs:485`
- linux — `brcmf_notify_connect_status`. In `brcmf_is_linkup`, status 6 with firmware supplicant in PSK or SAE mode sets `BRCMF_VIF_STATUS_EAP_SUCCESS`; link-up is reported once both EAP_SUCCESS and ASSOC_SUCCESS (from SET_SSID) are set. In `brcmf_is_nonetwork`, any status other than 6 ends the connect attempt with `brcmf_bss_connect_done(..., false)`; this test is not gated on `use_fwsup` — reads event_code, status; reason is not tested — `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6247-6270` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6294-6295` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6308-6313` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6836-6841`
- linux — `brcmf_bss_connect_done` acts only if `BRCMF_VIF_STATUS_CONNECTING` was set, so a non-6 PSK_SUP on an already connected interface changes nothing there `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6696-6697`
- linux — branch order: in AP mode the event goes to `brcmf_notify_connect_status_ap`, which tests only LINK, ASSOC_IND, REASSOC_IND, DISASSOC_IND, DEAUTH_IND and DEAUTH; `brcmf_is_linkdown` never matches PSK_SUP `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6796-6797` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6745-6773` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6280-6282`
- whd (receive path) — rewrites the header before dispatch for this event: status += `WLC_SUP_STATUS_OFFSET` (256), reason += `WLC_E_SUP_REASON_OFFSET` (512); same code in the msgbuf path — reads event_type, status, reason — `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:866-871` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:822-827` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:80` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:267`
- whd — `whd_wifi_join_events_handler`. Ignored unless `JOIN_LINK_READY` is already set. Status `WLC_SUP_KEYED` clears the security flags and sets `JOIN_SECURITY_COMPLETE`. Otherwise the join attempt is marked complete with one error flag: M1 timeout, M3 timeout, G1 timeout, or `JOIN_EAPOL_KEY_FAILURE` — reads bsscfgidx, status, reason (after the offsets are added); event data unused — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1349` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1351` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1359-1402`
- whd — the flags become the join result: `WHD_EAPOL_KEY_PACKET_M1_TIMEOUT`, `_M3_TIMEOUT`, `_G1_TIMEOUT`, `WHD_EAPOL_KEY_FAILURE`, or `WHD_SUCCESS` when `JOIN_SECURITY_COMPLETE` is set with the other join flags `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2190-2206`
- c-driver — status 6 sets `WIFI_JOIN_STATE_KEYED`. Status 4, 8 or 10 with reason 15 sets `pend_rejoin`, and the poll then calls `cyw43_ll_wifi_rejoin`. Anything else sets `wifi_join_state = WIFI_JOIN_STATE_BADAUTH` — reads status, reason — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:403-415` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:228-231`
- c-driver — for open networks the KEYED flag is pre-set at join time, because no PSK_SUP is expected `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:661-663`
- Header byte swaps: embassy swaps version, flags, event_type, status, reason, auth_type, datalen `embassy/structs.rs:315-323`; whd swaps flags, event_type, status, reason, auth_type, datalen `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:843-848`; c-driver swaps only flags, event_type, status, reason `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:738-741`
- Status values, raw, own numbering for this event (linux names): 4 FWSUP_WAIT_M1, 5 FWSUP_PREP_M2, 6 FWSUP_COMPLETED, 7 FWSUP_TIMEOUT, 8 FWSUP_WAIT_M3, 9 FWSUP_PREP_M4, 10 FWSUP_WAIT_G1, 11 FWSUP_PREP_G2 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:135-143`
- Status values, whd names, each defined as raw + 256: 0 DISCONNECTED, 1 CONNECTING, 2 IDREQUIRED, 3 AUTHENTICATING, 4 AUTHENTICATED = KEYXCHANGE_WAIT_M1, 5 KEYXCHANGE = KEYXCHANGE_PREP_M2, 6 KEYED, 7 TIMEOUT, 8 LAST_BASIC_STATE = KEYXCHANGE_WAIT_M3, 9 KEYXCHANGE_PREP_M4, 10 KEYXCHANGE_WAIT_G1, 11 KEYXCHANGE_PREP_G2 `whd/inc/whd_events.h:80` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:106-123`
- Reason values, raw: 0 OTHER, 1 DECRYPT_KEY_DATA, 2 BAD_UCAST_WEP128, 3 BAD_UCAST_WEP40, 4 UNSUP_KEY_LEN, 5 PW_KEY_CIPHER, 6 MSG3_TOO_MANY_IE, 7 MSG3_IE_MISMATCH, 8 NO_INSTALL_FLAG, 9 MSG3_NO_GTK, 10 GRP_KEY_CIPHER, 11 GRP_MSG1_NO_GTK, 12 GTK_DECRYPT_FAIL, 13 SEND_FAIL, 14 DEAUTH, 15 WPA_PSK_TMO; whd defines each as raw + 512 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:164-180` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:130-146` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:267` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:328-344`
- Reason values defined by linux only: 16 WPA_PSK_M1_TMO, 17 WPA_PSK_M3_TMO `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:181-182`
- Tested, status 6 = success: embassy `embassy/runner.rs:1278` (also needs flags 0 and reason 0), `embassy/control.rs:489`; linux `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6253`; whd `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1363`; c-driver `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:404`
- Tested, status != 6 = failure: linux `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6308-6309`
- Tested, status 4 with reason 0 ignored: embassy `embassy/control.rs:487`
- Tested, reason 14 ignored: embassy `embassy/runner.rs:1287`
- Tested, status 4, 8 or 10 with reason 15: c-driver rejoins `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:406`; whd sets a separate M1, M3 or G1 timeout flag `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1373-1392`
- Drivers disagree on status 4 with reason 15 (M1 timeout): embassy join fails with `AuthenticationFailure` `embassy/control.rs:486-492`; c-driver rejoins `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:406-410`; whd sets `JOIN_EAPOL_KEY_M1_TIMEOUT` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1373-1378`; linux fails the connect as for any non-6 status `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6308-6313`
- Source comments call it "WPA Handshake" `embassy/events.rs:102` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:85`; the whd public header describes it as a WPA handshake failure during association `whd/inc/whd_events.h:50`
- whd `whd_event_to_string` returns the name string for this code (`CASE_RETURN_STRING`) `whd/src/whd_utils.c:263`; the whd command log labels code 46, status 262 and reason 512 `whd/src/whd_utils.c:1098-1100` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:1119-1121` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:1128-1130`
- c-driver has it in its event name table `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:287`
- The PSK_SUP event published at `embassy/control.rs:1034` is test code `embassy/control.rs:1030-1036`

### 47 COUNTRY_CODE_CHANGED
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `COUNTRY_CODE_CHANGED` `embassy/events.rs:104` / linux `BRCMF_E_COUNTRY_CODE_CHANGED` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:71` / whd `WLC_E_COUNTRY_CODE_CHANGED` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:86` / c-driver: not found `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66`. Same spelling apart from the prefix. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask. The `bsscfg:event_msgs` mask sent at init is 24 bytes of 0xFF with only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG and ROAM cleared; this bit stays set `embassy/control.rs:305-319`<br>c-driver: default mask. 19 mask bytes of 0xff with only bits 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 cleared; this bit stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`<br>linux: not enabled. The mask is zeroed and only events with a registered handler, plus IF, get their bit `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456`; no `brcmf_fweh_register` call for this event found in linux. Until `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` runs (`linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8637`, `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8674`), preinit has only read the firmware's own `event_msgs` mask, set the IF bit and written it back; these sources do not show that firmware default `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`. The mask goes out as the named variable `event_msgs`, or as `event_msgs_ext` through the cyw vendor hook `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:458-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:79-99`<br>whd: not enabled by default. The mask is zeroed and only events in a registered handler list are set `whd/src/whd_events.c:408-419`; this event is in none of the internal lists `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`; an application can enable any code by passing its own list to `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`, which re-sends the mask `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-492` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:230` |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1357-1616` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1357-1616` |

- embassy — no event-specific handling: debug-logs the event, leaves link state alone (`_ => false` arm), and publishes it to the event queue only if the driver-side mask has it enabled; embassy enables only SET_SSID/AUTH/PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT there — tests no message fields; a published message would carry event_type, status and reason with `Payload::None` — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1345` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`
- c-driver — no handling: `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for this event type; dumped only when `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` is set — reads no fields — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:315-317`
- whd — no handling: a case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler`, in the block listed for gcc pedantic checking; the block falls through to `default`, which only calls `whd_assert(..., 0 != 0)` and breaks. That handler is registered for `join_events` only, which does not list this event — reads no fields — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1520` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2069`
- linux — defined in the event enum, no handler registered anywhere in linux — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:71`
- No driver tests status, reason or flags for this event `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1357-1616`
- c-driver has no `CYW43_EV_` define for this code `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66`
- whd `whd_event_to_string` lists this code with `CASE_RETURN`, which is defined as `break`, so the function returns the literal "Unknown", not the event name `whd/src/whd_utils.c:264` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:196-197` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:358`

### 54 IF
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `IF` `embassy/events.rs:117` / linux `BRCMF_E_IF` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:77` / whd `WLC_E_IF` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:91`, also logged as the string "WLC_E_IF" for code 54 `whd/src/whd_utils.c:1102-1105` / c-driver: not found (no `CYW43_EV_*` entry for this code) `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | embassy: explicitly cleared (`evts.unset(Event::IF)`) before writing `bsscfg:event_msgs` `embassy/control.rs:310-312` `embassy/control.rs:319`; the software mask never enables it either (the only enable calls are for SET_SSID/AUTH/PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT) `embassy/events.rs:345-348` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`; c-driver: explicitly cleared, `CLR_EV(buf, 54)` ("interface change") `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2056`; linux: enabled at preinit by reading `event_msgs`, setting the IF bit and writing it back `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`; set again whenever the mask is rebuilt, independent of handler registration `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:454-456`; a handler (`brcmf_notify_vif_event`) is also registered `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:7013-7014`; whd: in the `apsta_events` list { WLC_E_IF, WLC_E_LINK }, registered while a soft-AP interface is being created `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:280-281`; in the `vif_event` list { WLC_E_IF } of the optional WLANSENSE component, registered when its interface is created `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:285-286` |
| Handled by | linux `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:127-191` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6929-6975`; whd `whd/src/whd_ap.c:167-191` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:226-269`. embassy and c-driver have no handler `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` |
| Event data | 5 bytes, all u8: offset 0 ifidx, 1 action, 2 flags, 3 bsscfgidx, 4 role. linux `struct brcmf_if_event` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:279-285` and whd `wl_vif_event_t` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:4605-4611` are identical; whd's alternate header set defines the same record `whd/src/include_helium/whd_wlioctl.h:4772-4778`. embassy and c-driver do not parse it `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` |

- linux — `brcmf_fweh_handle_if_event`, special-cased before normal dispatch: validates the indexes, adds a netdev interface on ADD, resets the protocol layer on CHANGE, calls the registered handler, removes the interface on DEL when nobody is waiting — reads data as `brcmf_if_event` (action, ifidx, bsscfgidx, flags, role), plus the header ifname and addr on ADD — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:307-310` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:127-191`
- linux — IF is the one event queued even when no handler is registered — reads the event code — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`
- linux — `brcmf_notify_vif_event`: records the action, binds the pending vif to the new interface on ADD, wakes the waiter (`vif_wq`) for ADD / DEL / CHANGE — reads data: action (logs flags, ifidx, bsscfgidx) — `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6929-6975`
- whd — `whd_handle_apsta_event`: while soft-AP creation is waiting, a WLC_E_IF (or WLC_E_LINK) releases the wait semaphore — reads event_type only; data unused — `whd/src/whd_ap.c:167-191`
- whd — `whd_wlansense_handle_vif_event`: on action ADD creates the `wlansense0` interface with the event's bsscfgidx / ifidx and stores its role; any other action is logged as unhandled and the handler's return value is set to NULL — reads data as `wl_vif_event_t`: action, ifidx, bsscfgidx, role — `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:226-269` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:263-267`
- whd — elsewhere not handled: appears in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` only in the list of unexpected cases that fall to the default assert (it is not in `join_events`, so that handler is never registered for it), and in the event-name string table — reads event_type only — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1527` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:271`
- embassy — no specific handling: falls to the `_ => false` arm of the link-state match; would be published with `Payload::None` only if the software mask enabled it — reads no fields — `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — no branch for this code in `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`; with async-event tracing on it is printed by number (no name-table entry) — trace print reads flags, event_type, status, reason, interface — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-310`
- No driver tests the header status or reason for IF; the tests are on data bytes. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:127-191` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:226-269`
- action values: linux BRCMF_E_IF_ADD = 1, BRCMF_E_IF_DEL = 2, BRCMF_E_IF_CHANGE = 3 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:185-187`, tested at `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:164` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:178` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:184` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6945-6974`; whd WHD_IF_E_ADD = 1, WHD_IF_E_DEL = 2, WHD_IF_E_CHANGE = 3 `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.h:35-37`, ADD tested at `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:247`
- flags value: BRCMF_E_IF_FLAG_NOIF = 1 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:190`; linux ignores an event with NOIF set unless it is the P2P device `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:144-151`
- role values: STA = 0, AP = 1, WDS = 2, P2P_GO = 3, P2P_CLIENT = 4 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:193-197`; linux tests P2P_CLIENT and STA `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:145-146`
- Range checks: linux requires ifidx and bsscfgidx < BRCMF_MAX_IFS `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:152-160`
- linux callers wait for a given action with `brcmf_cfg80211_wait_vif_event` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8154-8161`: ADD at `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:855` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:2218` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:2342`; DEL at `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:1283` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:2439`; CHANGE at `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:2110`
- linux `brcmf_add_if` deletes a stale interface first in case a DEL event was missed `linux/brcmfmac/core.c:862-867`

|---|---|

- linux — `brcmf_p2p_notify_listen_complete`: if a discover-listen was in progress, wakes a waiter for the next action frame and reports `cfg80211_remain_on_channel_expired` — reads no message fields and no data (documented as unused); the function is also called directly with NULL arguments — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1037-1061` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1033-1034` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1075`

- embassy — no specific handling: falls to the `_ => false` arm of the link-state match; would be published with `Payload::None` only if the software mask enabled it — reads no fields — `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — no branch for this code in `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`; with async-event tracing on it is printed by number (no name-table entry) — trace print reads flags, event_type, status, reason, interface — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-310`
- No driver in these sources tests status, reason or flags for this event. `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1037-1061`

| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `RSSI` `embassy/events.rs:121` / linux `BRCMF_E_RSSI` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:79` / whd `WLC_E_RSSI` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:93` / c-driver: not found (no `CYW43_EV_*` entry for this code) `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | embassy: bit left set in the mask written with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (24 bytes of 0xFF; only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM cleared) `embassy/control.rs:305-319`; the driver-side software mask starts all-zero and nothing enables this event, so the runner never publishes it `embassy/events.rs:345-348` `embassy/runner.rs:1316`; c-driver: bit left set in the mask written with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (19 bytes of 0xff; only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 cleared) `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`; linux: enabled; handler `brcmf_notify_rssi` is registered `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:7027` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-452`. linux also configures levels first: it writes the named variable `rssi_event` (`struct brcmf_rssi_event_le`: rate_limit_msec, rssi_level_num, rssi_levels[]) with three levels `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:5801-5812` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:869-873`; BRCMF_MAX_RSSI_LEVELS = 8 `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:860`; whd: in no built-in handler list; the mask is zeroed and rebuilt from the registered handler lists, so it is enabled only if an application registers for it `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-416` |
| Handled by | linux `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6886-6927`. No handler in embassy, c-driver or whd `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` |
| Event data | linux only: `struct brcmf_rssi_be`, 12 bytes, big-endian: offset 0 rssi (`__be32`), offset 4 snr (`__be32`), offset 8 noise (`__be32`) `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:854-858`. linux also accepts a short form of only the 4-byte big-endian rssi when datalen is >= 4 and < 12 `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6894-6903`. No other driver parses the data `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1529` |

- linux — `brcmf_notify_rssi`: reads the new RSSI, stores it, and sends a cfg80211 CQM LOW notification if rssi <= low threshold or rssi == 0, HIGH if rssi > high threshold — reads datalen and data (rssi; snr and noise when present); status and reason not read — `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6886-6927`
- whd — not handled: appears in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` only in the list of unexpected cases that fall to the default assert (it is not in `join_events`, so that handler is never registered for it), and in the event-name string table — reads event_type only — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1529` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:273`
- embassy — no specific handling: falls to the `_ => false` arm of the link-state match; would be published with `Payload::None` only if the software mask enabled it — reads no fields — `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — no branch for this code in `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`; with async-event tracing on it is printed by number (no name-table entry) — trace print reads flags, event_type, status, reason, interface — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-310`
- No status, reason or flags tests. Data tests in linux: datalen >= 12, else datalen >= 4, else the event is rejected as too short `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6894-6903`; rssi <= cqm_rssi_low or rssi == 0 gives LOW, rssi > cqm_rssi_high gives HIGH `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6914-6924`
- linux reads this data with `be32_to_cpu`, unlike the little-endian records used for other event data, such as the escan result `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6895-6899` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:470-476`
- whd defines a separate WLC_E_RSSI_LQM = 133, a different event `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:161`

| Handled by | embassy `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1347` `embassy/control.rs:916-929`; c-driver `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:743-746` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:319-330`; linux `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3673-3783`; whd `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2582-3035` |
| Event data | Little-endian. 12-byte escan header, then one BSS record with a 128-byte fixed part, then its information elements; field offsets are in the layout bullets below. All four drivers parse it `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:470-476` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:556-563` `embassy/structs.rs:476-481` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:669-675` |

- embassy — runner, only if the software mask has the event enabled: for status PARTIAL parses `ScanResults` then `BssInfo` from the data and publishes `Payload::BssInfo`; if either parse fails the event is dropped without publishing; for any other status publishes `Payload::None` — reads status, reason (copied into the published `Status`), data — `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1347` `embassy/runner.rs:1321-1326`
- embassy — `Scanner::next`: returns the `BssInfo` for a PARTIAL event; any other status ends the scan (returns None) — reads status — `embassy/control.rs:916-929`
- c-driver — low level, `cyw43_ll_wifi_parse_scan_result`, for status PARTIAL: if ie_offset + ie_length > length it overwrites status with 0xFFFFFFFF and returns; otherwise walks the IEs for RSN and WPA vendor IEs to compute auth_mode, masks channel to 8 bits and writes auth_mode into the event buffer — reads event_type, status; data: bss.ie_offset, ie_length, length, capability, IEs — `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:743-746` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:668-718`
- c-driver — control layer, only while `wifi_scan_state == 1` (set when a scan starts): status 8 calls the user scan callback with the result; status 0 sets `wifi_scan_state = 2`; every other status, including 0xFFFFFFFF, is ignored — reads event_type, status, u.scan_result — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:319-330` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:607`
- linux — `brcmf_cfg80211_escan_handler`: ABORT is ignored; otherwise returns -EPERM unless a scan is marked busy; a PARTIAL result is length-checked, dropped if IBSS on a non-ad-hoc wiphy, de-duplicated and appended to the escan buffer; any other status ends the scan, reports the collected BSS list to cfg80211 and flags the scan aborted unless status is SUCCESS — reads status, datalen; data as `brcmf_escan_result_le`: buflen, bss_count, bss_info_le (length, capability, version) — `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3673-3783`
- whd — `whd_wifi_scan_events_handler`: returns at once if no scan callback is set; SUCCESS calls the callback with WHD_SCAN_COMPLETED_SUCCESSFULLY and deregisters; NEWSCAN / NEWASSOC / ABORT call it with WHD_SCAN_ABORTED and deregister; other non-PARTIAL statuses are ignored; PARTIAL validates the record and converts it to a `whd_scan_result_t` (channel: from a chanspec helper for a 6 GHz chanspec, else ctl_ch if n_cap, else the chanspec low byte; SSID, BSSID, RSSI, SNR, max data rate from the rateset and HT Capabilities IE, bss_type from capability, security from the RSN IE / WPA vendor IE / privacy bit, SAE-H2E flag from the RSNX IE, beacon flag, country code from the Country IE, band from chanspec), then calls the callback with WHD_SCAN_INCOMPLETE; if the callback cleared the result pointer the scan is reported aborted and the handler deregistered — reads status; data as `wl_escan_result_t`: bss_count, bss_info[0]: version, ie_offset, ie_length, length, SSID_len, SSID, BSSID, chanspec, n_cap, ctl_ch, flags, RSSI, SNR, rateset.count, rateset.rates, capability, beacon_period, and the IEs — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2582-3035` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3022-3023` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3026-3032`
- whd — in the join path not handled: appears in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` only in the list of unexpected cases that fall to the default assert (it is not in `join_events`, so that handler is never registered for it), and in the event-name string table — reads event_type only — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1541` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:286`
- Layout, escan header (12 bytes, little-endian; offset / size / field): 0 / 4 / buflen u32; 4 / 4 / version u32; 8 / 2 / sync_id u16 (read by no driver's handler); 10 / 2 / bss_count u16; 12 / 128 + IEs / one BSS record `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:470-476` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:556-563` `embassy/structs.rs:476-481` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:669-675`
- Layout, BSS record definitions (little-endian; offsets from the start of the record, add 12 for the offset in the event data): linux `struct brcmf_bss_info_le` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:314-348`; whd `wl_bss_info_t` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:926-964`; embassy `BssInfo` `embassy/structs.rs:507-569`; c-driver `cyw43_scan_result_internal_t` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:486-516`. The offsets in the next bullets follow embassy's explicit `packed(2)` field list with its reserved fields; embassy's layout test asserts ie_offset at 116, ie_length at 120, snr at 124 and size 128 against the linux record `embassy/structs.rs:507-585`. linux declares none of the pad bytes and whd declares none between ie_offset and ie_length or at the tail; in those two the pads come from the records' natural alignment (inferred from the declarations; neither is under a pack pragma) `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:314-348` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:926-964` `embassy/structs.rs:556-559`
- Layout, BSS record offsets 0-115 (offset / size / field): 0 / 4 / version u32; 4 / 4 / length u32; 8 / 6 / BSSID; 14 / 2 / beacon_period u16; 16 / 2 / capability u16; 18 / 1 / SSID_len u8; 19 / 32 / SSID; 51 / 1 / pad; 52 / 4 / rateset.count u32; 56 / 16 / rateset.rates; 72 / 2 / chanspec u16; 74 / 2 / atim_window u16; 76 / 1 / dtim_period u8; 77 / 1 / pad; 78 / 2 / RSSI i16 (linux declares it `__le16`); 80 / 1 / phy_noise i8; 81 / 1 / n_cap u8; 82 / 2 / pad; 84 / 4 / nbss_cap u32; 88 / 1 / ctl_ch u8; 89 / 3 / pad; 92 / 4 / reserved32 u32; 96 / 1 / flags u8; 97 / 1 / vht_cap u8; 98 / 2 / pad; 100 / 16 / basic_mcs `embassy/structs.rs:507-569` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:314-348`
- Layout, BSS record byte 97: vht_cap u8 in whd and embassy; linux and c-driver treat bytes 97-99 as reserved/pad `whd/inc/whd_types.h:926-964` `embassy/structs.rs:507-569` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:314-348` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:486-516`
- Layout, BSS record offsets 116-127 (offset / size / field): 116 / 2 / ie_offset u16, from the start of the record `embassy/structs.rs:555-567` `embassy/structs.rs:581-585` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:343`; 118 / 2 / pad (explicit in embassy and c-driver) `embassy/structs.rs:555-567` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:486-516`; 120 / 4 / ie_length u32 `embassy/structs.rs:555-567` `embassy/structs.rs:581-585` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:344`; 124 / 2 / SNR i16 (linux declares it `__le16`) `embassy/structs.rs:555-567` `embassy/structs.rs:581-585` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:345`; 126 / 2 / tail pad; fixed record size 128, header + record 140 `embassy/structs.rs:555-585` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3701-3717`
- Layout, information elements: at record + ie_offset for ie_length bytes `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2783-2787` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:684-685`
- Layout, c-driver application view: c-driver gives the application a re-labelled view, `cyw43_ev_scan_result_t`, overlaid on the event data from the escan header onward (event data starts at offset 48 of c-driver's event record). Offsets from the start of the event data: bssid 20, ssid_len 30, ssid 31, channel 84 (the chanspec field, which c-driver masks to 8 bits in place), rssi 90, auth_mode 104 (computed by c-driver and written over the record's reserved32 field) `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:222-234` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:237-249` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:675` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:714-715`
- status 8 (PARTIAL), one scan result in this event: linux BRCMF_E_STATUS_PARTIAL `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:126`, tested `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3699`; whd WLC_E_STATUS_PARTIAL `whd/inc/whd_events.h:69`, tested `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2633`; c-driver CYW43_STATUS_PARTIAL `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:77`, tested `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744` and as literal 8 at `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:321`; embassy `EStatus::PARTIAL`, tested `embassy/runner.rs:1320` `embassy/control.rs:919`
- status 0 (SUCCESS), scan complete: whd `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2615`; c-driver `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:327`. linux treats any status other than PARTIAL and ABORT as end of scan, and status != SUCCESS as aborted `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3769-3776`. embassy ends the scan on any non-PARTIAL status without distinguishing success from abort `embassy/control.rs:919-922`
- status 4 (ABORT): linux ignores the event `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3690-3691`; whd reports WHD_SCAN_ABORTED for ABORT (4), NEWSCAN (9) and NEWASSOC (10) `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2623-2624` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:65` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:70` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:71`
- No driver tests reason or the header flags for this event. `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1347` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:319-330` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3673-3783` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2582-3035`
- linux data checks: datalen >= 140, buflen <= BRCMF_ESCAN_BUF_SIZE, buflen <= datalen, buflen >= 140 `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3701-3717`; bss_count must be 1 `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3718`; bss_info length must equal buflen - 12 `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3733-3734`; WL_ESCAN_RESULTS_FIXED_SIZE = 12 `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:478-479`
- whd data checks: bss_info version must be WL_BSS_INFO_VERSION = 109 `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2642-2651` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:493`; bss_count must be 1 `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2653-2657`; rejects SSID_len > 32, ie_offset < 128 (`sizeof(wl_bss_info_t)`) or ie_offset > 128 + ie_length `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2664-2671`; rejects ie_offset > length or ie_length > length - ie_offset `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2783-2787`; drops results without flag WL_BSS_FLAGS_RSSI_ONCHANNEL = 0x04 `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2707-2712` `whd/src/include/whd_types_int.h:57`; flag WL_BSS_FLAGS_FROM_BEACON = 0x01 marks a result as from a beacon `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2996-2999` `whd/src/include/whd_types_int.h:55`
- c-driver data check: ie_offset + ie_length <= length, else status is overwritten with 0xFFFFFFFF so the control layer sees neither 8 nor 0 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:677-680` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:321-327`
- embassy data checks: `ScanResults::parse` needs 12 bytes and requires a full `BssInfo` only when bss_count > 0 `embassy/structs.rs:485-496`; `BssInfo::parse` requires 128 bytes `embassy/structs.rs:602-605`; embassy does not check bss_count == 1, version or the IE bounds `embassy/runner.rs:1320-1327`
- embassy declares the padding explicitly: `reserved6` (2 bytes between ie_offset and ie_length) and `reserved7` (2-byte tail) `embassy/structs.rs:556-567`
- whd's alternate header set has the same `wl_escan_result_t` and WL_BSS_INFO_VERSION 109 `whd/src/include_helium/whd_wlioctl.h:555-562` `whd/src/include_helium/whd_wlioctl.h:492`

### 70 ACTION_FRAME_OFF_CHAN_COMPLETE
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `ACTION_FRAME_OFF_CHAN_COMPLETE` `embassy/events.rs:148` / linux `BRCMF_E_ACTION_FRAME_OFF_CHAN_COMPLETE` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:90` / whd `WLC_E_ACTION_FRAME_OFF_CHAN_COMPLETE` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:106` / c-driver: not found (no `CYW43_EV_*` entry for this code) `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | embassy: bit left set in the mask written with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (24 bytes of 0xFF; only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM cleared) `embassy/control.rs:305-319`; the driver-side software mask starts all-zero and nothing enables this event, so the runner never publishes it `embassy/events.rs:345-348` `embassy/runner.rs:1316`; c-driver: bit left set in the mask written with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (19 bytes of 0xff; only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 cleared) `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`; linux: enabled; handler `brcmf_p2p_notify_action_tx_complete` is registered (same handler as code 60) `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:7023-7024` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-452`; whd: in no built-in handler list; the mask is zeroed and rebuilt from the registered handler lists, so it is enabled only if an application registers for it `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-416` |
| Handled by | linux `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1522-1555`. No handler in embassy, c-driver or whd `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1519` |

- linux — `brcmf_p2p_notify_action_tx_complete`: if an action frame send is in progress, this event unconditionally completes `send_af_done` (the branch for any code other than ACTION_FRAME_COMPLETE) — reads event_code; status is only logged — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1522-1555` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1551-1553` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1529-1532`
- whd — not handled: appears in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` only in the list of unexpected cases that fall to the default assert (it is not in `join_events`, so that handler is never registered for it), and in the event-name string table — reads event_type only — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1542` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:287`
- embassy — no specific handling: falls to the `_ => false` arm of the link-state match; would be published with `Payload::None` only if the software mask enabled it — reads no fields — `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — no branch for this code in `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`; with async-event tracing on it is printed by number (no name-table entry) — trace print reads flags, event_type, status, reason, interface — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-310`
- No status or reason value is tested for this event. `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1551-1553`
- whd's definition carries a note that this code used to be WLC_E_WAKE_EVENT `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:106`
- A linux comment spells it WLC_E_ACTION_FRAME_OFFCHAN_COMPLETE, which is not a defined name in any of these drivers `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1546`

### 71 PROBRESP_MSG
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `PROBRESP_MSG` `embassy/events.rs:150` / linux `BRCMF_E_PROBERESP_MSG` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:91` / whd `WLC_E_PROBRESP_MSG` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:107` / c-driver: not found (no `CYW43_EV_*` entry for this code) `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | embassy: explicitly cleared (`evts.unset(Event::PROBRESP_MSG)`, on two consecutive lines) before writing `bsscfg:event_msgs` `embassy/control.rs:315-316` `embassy/control.rs:319`; the software mask never enables it either `embassy/events.rs:345-348` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`; c-driver: explicitly cleared, `CLR_EV(buf, 71)` ("probe response") `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2057`; linux: not enabled; the mask is zeroed and a bit is set only for a code with a registered handler, and no registration for this code was found `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-452`; whd: in the `join_events` list, registered with `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` for the duration of a join `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2069` |
| Handled by | whd `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1473-1475`. No handler in embassy, c-driver or linux `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498-499` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1473-1475` |

- whd — `whd_wifi_join_events_handler`: sets the JOIN_PROBE_RESPONSE bit (bit 10) in the join status for the event's bsscfgidx; later join-status decoding includes that bit in its cases — reads bsscfgidx; status, reason and data not read — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1473-1475` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:71` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2190-2205`
- whd — event-name string table entry only — reads event_type — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:288`
- linux — no handler registered; `brcmf_fweh_process_event` drops any event other than IF whose handler slot is empty — reads event code only — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498-499`
- embassy — no specific handling: falls to the `_ => false` arm of the link-state match; would be published with `Payload::None` only if the software mask enabled it — reads no fields — `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — no branch for this code in `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`; with async-event tracing on it is printed by number (no name-table entry) — trace print reads flags, event_type, status, reason, interface — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-310`
- No status or reason value is tested: whd sets its flag on any occurrence. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1473-1475`
- Spelling differs: linux is PROBERESP (extra E); embassy and whd are PROBRESP `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:91` `embassy/events.rs:150` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:107`

### 72 P2P_PROBREQ_MSG
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `P2P_PROBREQ_MSG` `embassy/events.rs:152` / whd `WLC_E_P2P_PROBREQ_MSG` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:108` / linux `BRCMF_E_P2P_PROBEREQ_MSG` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:92` / c-driver: not found — the `CYW43_EV_` list has no entry for this code `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66`. Spelling differs: embassy and whd write PROBREQ, linux writes PROBEREQ (extra E) for the same code 72; the comment above the linux record `brcmf_rx_mgmt_data` still uses the `WLC_E_P2P_PROBREQ_MSG` spelling `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:715`. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask — bit left set in the mask sent with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (24 bytes of 0xFF; only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM are cleared) `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. Host side, embassy queues an event only if its own mask has it enabled; the driver enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so this one is dropped after the debug log unless an application enables it `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask — bit left set (19 mask bytes of 0xff; only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared) in the `bsscfg:event_msgs` write `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. whd: not enabled — not in `join_events`, `scan_events` or `auth_events` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-163`; whd sets mask bits only for the events of registered handlers `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-417`. linux: enabled — `brcmf_p2p_notify_rx_mgmt_p2p_probereq` is registered, and every event with a handler gets its mask bit `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:7015-7016`. The mask goes out as `event_msgs` unless the vendor hook takes over; the cyw vendor module writes it as `event_msgs_ext` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:79-99`. |
| Handled by | linux `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1956-2020`. No handler in whd, embassy, c-driver. |
| Event data | linux: record `brcmf_rx_mgmt_data` (linux), 16 bytes, all fields big-endian: version be16 @0 (2); chanspec be16 @2 (2); rssi be32 @4 (4); mactime be32 @8 (4); rate be32 @12 (4) `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:718-724`. Offsets and size are from the field order of fixed-width types, no padding (computed). Frame bytes start at offset 16; length = datalen - 16. linux reads only chanspec from the header. For this event linux passes the bytes after the header straight to `cfg80211_rx_mgmt` as a management frame, so they are a complete 802.11 probe request including its MAC header (inferred from that use) `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:2007-2014`. whd, embassy, c-driver: no parsing. whd names a header `wl_event_rx_frame_data_t` only in the comments for codes 75 and 137, not for code 72; a definition of it is not found in these sources `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:110` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:165`. |

- linux — `brcmf_p2p_notify_rx_mgmt_p2p_probereq`: decodes the channel from the rx header; if the driver is searching for a common channel and the sender is the awaited peer, records the peer channel and completes the wait; drops the event for bsscfgidx 0, during GO negotiation, or if user space has not registered for probe requests; otherwise passes the frame to `cfg80211_rx_mgmt` — reads event_code and reason (debug print only), datalen, addr, bsscfgidx, data (chanspec, then the frame at offset 16) — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1956-2020` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1971` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1974` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1979` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1983` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1992` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:2007-2008`
- linux — tests no status or reason value; it tests bsscfgidx == 0 (return without forwarding) and compares addr with the awaited peer address — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1990-1993` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1982-1983`
- linux — a comment in the handler says the firmware sends two copies, one per bsscfg index, and that only those with a non-zero index are passed up — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1990-1991`
- linux — uses the same header record in the cyw vendor handler `brcmf_notify_auth_frame_rx` for a different event, EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:274-283`
- whd — not handled: a fall-through case of `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that ends in `whd_assert` ("Received event which was not registered") — reads no message field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1543` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615`
- embassy — no specific handling: decoded to the `Event` enum and debug-logged; matches no arm of the link-state match; if host-enabled it is published with `Payload::None` — event_type, status, reason are copied into the published status; data is not parsed — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — not handled: only the optional trace dump sees it (no name table entry, so it is printed as a number); `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for it — flags, event_type, status, reason, interface are printed only when `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` is set — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-304` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:319-420`
- c-driver — before its callback, `cyw43_ll_parse_async_event` byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason and has type-specific parsing for ESCAN_RESULT only — `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-746`
- whd — `whd_event_to_string` has a case for it but returns "Unknown": `CASE_RETURN` only breaks out of the switch — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:289` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:196-197` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:358`
- whd — header comment: "P2P Probe request received"; embassy carries the same text as a doc comment — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:108` `embassy/events.rs:151`

| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `FIFO_CREDIT_MAP` `embassy/events.rs:155` / whd `WLC_E_FIFO_CREDIT_MAP` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:109` / linux `BRCMF_E_FIFO_CREDIT_MAP` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:94` / c-driver: not found — the `CYW43_EV_` list has no entry for this code `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66`. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask — bit left set in the mask sent with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (24 bytes of 0xFF; only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM are cleared) `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. Host side, embassy queues an event only if its own mask has it enabled; the driver enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so this one is dropped after the debug log unless an application enables it `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask — bit left set (19 mask bytes of 0xff; only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared) in the `bsscfg:event_msgs` write `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. whd: not enabled — not in `join_events`, `scan_events` or `auth_events` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-163`; whd sets mask bits only for the events of registered handlers `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-417`. linux: conditional — `brcmf_fws_attach` (called from the BCDC protocol init) returns before registering the handler when fcmode is `BRCMF_FWS_FCMODE_NONE` and the bus does not set `always_use_fws_queue` `linux/brcmfmac/bcdc.c:430` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:2357-2362`. fcmode comes from the `fcmode` module parameter; only the USB bus sets `always_use_fws_queue` `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:58-60` `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:530` `linux/brcmfmac/usb.c:1272`. Otherwise `brcmf_fws_notify_credit_map` is registered and its mask bit is set `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:2379-2380`. The mask goes out as `event_msgs` unless the vendor hook takes over; the cyw vendor module writes it as `event_msgs_ext` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:79-99`. |

| Event data | linux: array of u8 credit counts at offset 0, one byte per FIFO, no endianness `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1602` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1613-1615`. linux requires datalen >= `BRCMF_FWS_FIFO_COUNT` (6): index 0 AC_BK, 1 AC_BE, 2 AC_VI, 3 AC_VO, 4 BCMC, 5 ATIM `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1604` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.h:21-30`. linux treats each byte as the total credit for that FIFO and adds the difference from the previous map `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1614-1615`. whd and embassy comment the layout as "[AC0,AC1,AC2,AC3,BC_MC,ATIM]" but do not parse it `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:109` `embassy/events.rs:154`. c-driver: no parsing. |

- linux — `brcmf_fws_notify_credit_map`: adjusts `fifo_credit[i]` by the change against the previously announced value, updates the per-FIFO bitmap `fifo_credit_map`, sets `creditmap_received`, schedules the dequeue worker — reads datalen and data (u8 `credits[i]`, loop over `ARRAY_SIZE(fws->fifo_credit)` entries); status, reason, flags, addr not read — `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1595-1627` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1602` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1604` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1614-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:493`

- c-driver — not handled: only the optional trace dump sees it (no name table entry, so it is printed as a number); `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for it — flags, event_type, status, reason, interface are printed only when `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` is set — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-304` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:319-420`
- c-driver — before its callback, `cyw43_ll_parse_async_event` byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason and has type-specific parsing for ESCAN_RESULT only — `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-746`

### 75 ACTION_FRAME_RX
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `ACTION_FRAME_RX` `embassy/events.rs:157` / whd `WLC_E_ACTION_FRAME_RX` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:110` / linux `BRCMF_E_ACTION_FRAME_RX` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:95` / c-driver: not found — the `CYW43_EV_` list has no entry for this code `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66`. embassy also defines `ACTION_FRAME_RX_NDIS` = 90, a different event `embassy/events.rs:183`. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask — bit left set in the mask sent with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (24 bytes of 0xFF; only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM are cleared) `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. Host side, embassy queues an event only if its own mask has it enabled; the driver enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so this one is dropped after the debug log unless an application enables it `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask — bit left set (19 mask bytes of 0xff; only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared) in the `bsscfg:event_msgs` write `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. whd: not enabled — not in `join_events`, `scan_events` or `auth_events` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-163`; whd sets mask bits only for the events of registered handlers `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-417`. linux: enabled — `brcmf_p2p_notify_action_frame_rx` is registered, and every event with a handler gets its mask bit `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:7019-7020`. The mask goes out as `event_msgs` unless the vendor hook takes over; the cyw vendor module writes it as `event_msgs_ext` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:79-99`. |
| Handled by | linux `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1414-1511`. No handler in whd, embassy, c-driver. |
| Event data | linux: record `brcmf_rx_mgmt_data` (linux), 16 bytes, all fields big-endian: version be16 @0 (2); chanspec be16 @2 (2); rssi be32 @4 (4); mactime be32 @8 (4); rate be32 @12 (4) `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:718-724`. Offsets and size are from the field order of fixed-width types, no padding (computed). Frame bytes start at offset 16; length = datalen - 16. linux reads only chanspec from the header. For this event the bytes after the header are the action frame body only, starting at the category byte: linux prepends its own 802.11 header and copies the data to `mgmt_frame->u.body`, so no MAC header is in the data (inferred from that use) `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1487-1499`. Body views used by linux: record `brcmf_p2p_pub_act_frame` — category u8 @0, action u8 @1, oui u8[3] @2, oui_type u8 @5, subtype u8 @6, dialog_token u8 @7, elts @8 `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:152-160`; record `brcmf_p2psd_gas_pub_act_frame` — category u8 @0, action u8 @1, dialog_token u8 @2, query_data @3 `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:189-194`. whd names the header `wl_event_rx_frame_data_t` in its comment; a definition of it is not found in these sources, so whether it matches linux's 16-byte record is not shown `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:110`. whd, embassy, c-driver: no parsing. |

- linux — `brcmf_p2p_notify_action_frame_rx`: decodes the channel; returns if user space has not registered for action frames; inspects P2P public and GAS action subtypes to drive P2P state (GO negotiation collision, mpc restore, next-frame wait); builds an `ieee80211_mgmt` header (da = own MAC, sa = addr, bssid from `BRCMF_C_GET_BSSID`) in front of the body and calls `cfg80211_rx_mgmt` — reads datalen, addr, data (chanspec, then the body at offset 16); status, reason, flags not read — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1414-1511` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1423` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1425` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1434` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1439` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1454` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1457` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1496`
- linux — tests no status or reason value; in the frame body it tests subtype == `P2P_PAF_GON_REQ` (0), `P2P_PAF_GON_CONF` (2), `P2P_PAF_PROVDIS_RSP` (8) — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1453` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1466-1469` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:70-78`
- linux — GAS test: category 0x04 with action 0x0a, 0x0b, 0x0c or 0x0d — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:312-318` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:88-92`
- linux — P2P public action test (`brcmf_p2p_is_pub_action`): category == `WLAN_CATEGORY_PUBLIC`, action == `WLAN_PUB_ACTION_VENDOR_SPECIFIC`, oui_type == `WLAN_OUI_TYPE_WFA_P2P`, and the 3 oui bytes read big-endian == `WLAN_OUI_WFA`: category 4, action 9, oui 50 6f 9a (0x506f9a), oui_type 9 — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:229-232` `linux-headers/ieee80211.h:1849` `linux-headers/ieee80211.h:1969` `linux-headers/ieee80211.h:2276-2277`
- linux — consumes a `P2P_PAF_GON_REQ` frame only if `brcmf_p2p_gon_req_collision()` also returns true — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1453-1464`
- linux — computes the frame length as datalen - 16 before it checks datalen >= 16 — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1423` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1434`
- linux — the doc comment says the event message is not used, but the handler reads datalen and addr — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1410`
- linux — difference from code 72, same 16-byte header: linux forwards the code 72 data as a whole frame and adds the MAC header itself for code 75. Both are inferred from linux's use of the bytes — `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:2007-2014` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1487-1499`
- linux — uses the same header record in the cyw vendor handler `brcmf_notify_auth_frame_rx` for a different event, EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:274-283`
- whd — not handled: a fall-through case of `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that ends in `whd_assert` ("Received event which was not registered") — reads no message field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1546` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615`
- embassy — no specific handling: decoded to the `Event` enum and debug-logged; matches no arm of the link-state match; if host-enabled it is published with `Payload::None` — event_type, status, reason are copied into the published status; data is not parsed — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — not handled: only the optional trace dump sees it (no name table entry, so it is printed as a number); `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for it — flags, event_type, status, reason, interface are printed only when `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` is set — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-304` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:319-420`
- c-driver — before its callback, `cyw43_ll_parse_async_event` byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason and has type-specific parsing for ESCAN_RESULT only — `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-746`

- whd — `whd_event_to_string` has a case for it but returns "Unknown": `CASE_RETURN` only breaks out of the switch — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:293` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:196-197` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:358`
- whd — header comment: "Wake Event timer fired, used for wake WLAN test mode"; embassy carries the same text as a doc comment — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:111` `embassy/events.rs:158`

### 80 CSA_COMPLETE_IND
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `CSA_COMPLETE_IND` `embassy/events.rs:166` / whd `WLC_E_CSA_COMPLETE_IND` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:115` / c-driver `CYW43_EV_CSA_COMPLETE_IND` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:64` / linux: not found — the `BRCMF_ENUM_DEF` list goes from ACTION_FRAME_RX (75) straight to TDLS_PEER_EVENT (92) `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:95-96`. Same spelling in embassy, whd and c-driver. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask — bit left set in the mask sent with `bsscfg:event_msgs` (24 bytes of 0xFF; only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM are cleared) `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. Host side, embassy queues an event only if its own mask has it enabled; the driver enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so this one is dropped after the debug log unless an application enables it `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask — bit left set (19 mask bytes of 0xff; only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared) in the `bsscfg:event_msgs` write `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. c-driver also names it (EV_CSA_COMPLETE_IND=80) in a comment listing events for a join-time `bsscfg:event_msgs` write that is commented out `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294`. whd: enabled while a join handler is registered — it is in `join_events[]`, and whd sets a mask bit for every event of every registered handler `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:2069` `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-417`. linux: not enabled — no handler is registered; only events with a registered handler (plus IF) get a mask bit `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456`. |
| Handled by | whd `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1455-1462` (log only). c-driver: name table entry only `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:289`. No handler in linux, embassy. |
| Event data | whd: record `wl_chan_switch_t` (whd) — mode u8 @0; count u8 @1; chspec u16 @2, read with `dtoh16` (little-endian); reg u8 @4; sizeof 6 with one trailing pad byte `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:3464-3470` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:918` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1460`. Offsets and size are from natural alignment (computed): the record is not inside a `#pragma pack(1)` region (the first in that file starts later) `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:3864`; a packed build would give the same offsets and size 5 (inferred). whd requires datalen >= sizeof(wl_chan_switch_t) and reads only chspec, taking the low 8 bits as the channel `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1456-1460`. linux, c-driver, embassy: no parsing. |

- whd — `whd_wifi_join_events_handler`: if datalen >= sizeof(wl_chan_switch_t), casts the data to `wl_chan_switch_t` and prints the channel at INFO level; no state change; the print compiles away unless `WPRINT_WHD_INFO` is enabled — reads event_type, bsscfgidx (bounds check), datalen, data (chspec, low 8 bits after `dtoh16`) — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1455-1462` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1351` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1459`
- whd — tests only datalen >= sizeof(wl_chan_switch_t); no driver tests a status, reason or flag value — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1456`
- whd — after the switch, `whd_wifi_is_ready_to_transceive` is evaluated as for every join event — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1618-1621`
- whd — uses the same record as the set buffer of the named variable (iovar) `csa` in `whd_wifi_set_channel` for the AP role: chspec written with `htod16`, count = 1, mode = 1, reg = 0 — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:638-649`
- whd — an identical record definition is in the helium include tree — `whd/src/include_helium/whd_wlioctl.h:3623-3629`
- whd — `whd_event_to_string` returns the name string for this event (`CASE_RETURN_STRING`) — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:297` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:193-194`
- embassy — no specific handling: decoded to the `Event` enum and debug-logged; matches no arm of the link-state match; if host-enabled it is published with `Payload::None` — event_type, status, reason are copied into the published status; data is not parsed — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1331`
- c-driver — name only: entry in `cyw43_async_event_name_table`, used by `cyw43_dump_async_event` when `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` is set; `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for it — flags, event_type, status, reason, interface are printed only — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:289` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-317` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:319-420`
- c-driver — before its callback, `cyw43_ll_parse_async_event` byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason and has type-specific parsing for ESCAN_RESULT only — `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-746`
- linux — not handled: `brcmf_fweh_process_event` drops an event with no registered handler — reads no message field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498-499`
- whd, embassy — no descriptive comment for this event; where the whd line has a trailing comment it belongs to the last enumerator on that line — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:115` `embassy/events.rs:166`

### 90 ACTION_FRAME_RX_NDIS
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `ACTION_FRAME_RX_NDIS` = 90 `embassy/events.rs:183` / whd `WLC_E_ACTION_FRAME_RX_NDIS` = 90, in the internal header only, not found in the public header `whd/inc/whd_events.h` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:121` / linux: not found — the event list goes from 75 to 92 to 127 with no value 90 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:95-97` / c-driver: not found — the `CYW43_EV_*` list has 17 defines, highest 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` / pico-sdk: not found — its `cybt_shared_bus*` files define no event codes `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus.c:1-421` `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus_driver.c:1-705` `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus_driver.h:1-81`. No spelling differences between embassy and whd. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask. Init sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 24 mask bytes of 255 (0xFF) and clears only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM; bit 90 (byte 11, bit 2) stays set `embassy/control.rs:307` `embassy/control.rs:311-317` `embassy/control.rs:319`. embassy also has a host-side mask that gates publishing; the only `enable()` calls are for SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so the driver itself never enables 90 there `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask. `cyw43_ll_wifi_on` sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 19 mask bytes of 255 (0xff) and clears only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71; bit 90 (byte 11, bit 2) stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2053-2057` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2060`. linux: not enabled. The mask starts zeroed and a bit is set only for a code with a registered handler, plus `BRCMF_E_IF` which is always set; linux has no enum value for 90, so no handler can be registered for it `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-450` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:456`. whd: not enabled by whd itself. The mask starts zeroed and bits are set only for events in a registered handler list; `WLC_E_ACTION_FRAME_RX_NDIS` is in none of whd's own lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-416` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable any raw event number through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`; no caller exists in these sources `whd/src/whd_events.c:443` `whd/src/whd_events.c:510-512`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; the event is dropped in embassy at `embassy/runner.rs:1316`, in c-driver at `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312`, in linux at `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`, in whd at `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no record for this event's data found in embassy, c-driver, linux or whd. embassy maps it to `Payload::None` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`; c-driver parses event data only for ESCAN_RESULT `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`; linux returns before any handler sees the data `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`; whd passes data only to a registered handler `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` |

- embassy — no handler. The event type is cast to u8 and decoded, debug-logged, falls through the link-state match, and is not published because the driver never enables its host-side mask bit; the default payload arm is `Payload::None` — reads no field specific to this event (the generic path reads event_type, status, flags, reason, auth_type) — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`
- c-driver — no handler. `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for event type 90. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` set it is printed as the bare number, because the name table has 89 entries (0..88) — the trace print shows flags, event_type, status, reason, interface; nothing else is read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:303` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`
- linux — no handler. `brcmf_fweh_process_event` returns for a code with no registered handler. Code 90 is below the per-vendor code count (cyw 197, bca 212, wcc 213), so it passes the range check and is dropped at the handler check — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- whd — no handler. Both dispatch paths (CDC/BDC and msgbuf) call a handler only when the event type is in that handler's registered list; `WLC_E_ACTION_FRAME_RX_NDIS` is in none — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865`
- whd — the name appears elsewhere only as a name string in `whd_event_to_string` and as a case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that falls into the default arm, which asserts "Received event which was not registered"; a source comment says the labels are there so that gcc's pedantic checks do not complain — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:307` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1560` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1614` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1340` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477`
- all drivers — status, reason and flags are not tested for this event; each driver drops it before any such test — `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912`
- whd, embassy — source comment, same text in both: "rx action frame event for NDIS only" — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:121` `embassy/events.rs:182`
- embassy, c-driver — both leave this bit set in the mask they send, so they request the event and then ignore it (inferred from the mask code; no driver shows the event arriving) — `embassy/control.rs:307` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051`

The mask starts zeroed and bits are set only for events in a registered handler list; `WLC_E_SPEEDY_RECREATE_FAIL` is in none of whd's own lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-416` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable any raw event number through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`; no caller exists in these sources `whd/src/whd_events.c:443` `whd/src/whd_events.c:510-512`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; the event is dropped in embassy at `embassy/runner.rs:1316`, in c-driver at `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312`, in linux at `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`, in whd at `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no record for this event's data found in embassy, c-driver, linux or whd. embassy maps it to `Payload::None` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`; c-driver parses event data only for ESCAN_RESULT `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`; linux returns before any handler sees the data `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`; whd passes data only to a registered handler `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` |

- embassy — no handler. The event type is cast to u8 and decoded, debug-logged, falls through the link-state match, and is not published because the driver never enables its host-side mask bit; the default payload arm is `Payload::None` — reads no field specific to this event (the generic path reads event_type, status, flags, reason, auth_type) — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`
- c-driver — no handler. `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for event type 93. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` set it is printed as the bare number, because the name table has 89 entries (0..88) — the trace print shows flags, event_type, status, reason, interface; nothing else is read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:303` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`
- linux — no handler. `brcmf_fweh_process_event` returns for a code with no registered handler. Code 93 is below the per-vendor code count (cyw 197, bca 212, wcc 213), so it passes the range check and is dropped at the handler check — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- whd — no handler. Both dispatch paths (CDC/BDC and msgbuf) call a handler only when the event type is in that handler's registered list; `WLC_E_SPEEDY_RECREATE_FAIL` is in none — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865`
- whd — the name appears elsewhere only as a name string in `whd_event_to_string` and as a case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that falls into the default arm, which asserts "Received event which was not registered"; a source comment says the labels are there so that gcc's pedantic checks do not complain — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:310` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1563` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1614` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1340` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477`
- all drivers — status, reason and flags are not tested for this event; each driver drops it before any such test — `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912`
- whd, embassy — source comment, same text in both: "fast assoc recreation failed" — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:125` `embassy/events.rs:186`
- embassy — code 92 is absent from the enum (91 is followed by 93); see 92 in the extra-events chapter — `embassy/events.rs:185` `embassy/events.rs:187`
- linux, whd — linux defines 92 as `BRCMF_E_TDLS_PEER_EVENT`; whd defines 92 twice, as `WLC_E_TDLS_PEER_EVENT` and `WLC_E_MESH_DHCP_SUCCESS` — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:96` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:123-124`
- embassy, c-driver — both leave this bit set in the mask they send, so they request the event and then ignore it (inferred from the mask code; no driver shows the event arriving) — `embassy/control.rs:307` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051`

| Names | embassy `AWDL_ROLE` = 97 `embassy/events.rs:195` / whd `WLC_E_AWDL_ROLE` = 97, in the internal header only, not found in the public header `whd/inc/whd_events.h` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:129` / linux: not found — the event list goes from 75 to 92 to 127 with no value 97 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:95-97` / c-driver: not found — the `CYW43_EV_*` list has 17 defines, highest 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` / pico-sdk: not found — its `cybt_shared_bus*` files define no event codes `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus.c:1-421` `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus_driver.c:1-705` `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus_driver.h:1-81`. No spelling differences between embassy and whd. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask. Init sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 24 mask bytes of 255 (0xFF) and clears only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM; bit 97 (byte 12, bit 1) stays set `embassy/control.rs:307` `embassy/control.rs:311-317` `embassy/control.rs:319`. embassy also has a host-side mask that gates publishing; the only `enable()` calls are for SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so the driver itself never enables 97 there `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask. `cyw43_ll_wifi_on` sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 19 mask bytes of 255 (0xff) and clears only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71; bit 97 (byte 12, bit 1) stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2053-2057` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2060`. linux: not enabled. The mask starts zeroed and a bit is set only for a code with a registered handler, plus `BRCMF_E_IF` which is always set; linux has no enum value for 97, so no handler can be registered for it `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-450` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:456`. whd: not enabled by whd itself. The mask starts zeroed and bits are set only for events in a registered handler list; `WLC_E_AWDL_ROLE` is in none of whd's own lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-416` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable any raw event number through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`; no caller exists in these sources `whd/src/whd_events.c:443` `whd/src/whd_events.c:510-512`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; the event is dropped in embassy at `embassy/runner.rs:1316`, in c-driver at `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312`, in linux at `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`, in whd at `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no record for this event's data found in embassy, c-driver, linux or whd. embassy maps it to `Payload::None` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`; c-driver parses event data only for ESCAN_RESULT `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`; linux returns before any handler sees the data `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`; whd passes data only to a registered handler `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` |

- embassy — no handler. The event type is cast to u8 and decoded, debug-logged, falls through the link-state match, and is not published because the driver never enables its host-side mask bit; the default payload arm is `Payload::None` — reads no field specific to this event (the generic path reads event_type, status, flags, reason, auth_type) — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`
- c-driver — no handler. `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for event type 97. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` set it is printed as the bare number, because the name table has 89 entries (0..88) — the trace print shows flags, event_type, status, reason, interface; nothing else is read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:303` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`
- linux — no handler. `brcmf_fweh_process_event` returns for a code with no registered handler. Code 97 is below the per-vendor code count (cyw 197, bca 212, wcc 213), so it passes the range check and is dropped at the handler check — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- whd — no handler. Both dispatch paths (CDC/BDC and msgbuf) call a handler only when the event type is in that handler's registered list; `WLC_E_AWDL_ROLE` is in none — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865`
- whd — the name appears elsewhere only as a name string in `whd_event_to_string` and as a case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that falls into the default arm, which asserts "Received event which was not registered"; a source comment says the labels are there so that gcc's pedantic checks do not complain — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:314` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1567` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1614` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1340` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477`
- all drivers — status, reason and flags are not tested for this event; each driver drops it before any such test — `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912`
- whd, embassy — source comment, same text in both: "AWDL Master/Slave/NE master role event" — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:129` `embassy/events.rs:194`
- embassy, c-driver — both leave this bit set in the mask they send, so they request the event and then ignore it (inferred from the mask code; no driver shows the event arriving) — `embassy/control.rs:307` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051`
