- whd — a header comment states that NAN sub-event numbers arrive in the reason field of `WLC_E_NAN`, and the enum defines them. No code tests them — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:288`
- whd — in `whd_event_reason_t`: `WLC_E_NAN_EVENT_STATUS_CHG` 9, `_MERGE` 10, `_STOP` 11, `_P2P` 12 — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:289-292`
- whd — in `whd_event_reason_t`: `_WINDOW_BEGIN_P2P` 13, `_WINDOW_BEGIN_MESH` 14, `_WINDOW_BEGIN_IBSS` 15, `_WINDOW_BEGIN_RANGING` 16, `_POST_DISC` 17, `_DATA_IF_ADD` 18, `_DATA_PEER_ADD` 19 — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:295-299`
- whd — in `whd_event_reason_t`: `_DATA_IND` 20, `_DATA_CONF` 21, `_SDF_RX` 22, `_DATA_END` 23, `_BCN_RX` 24 — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:302-305`
- whd — in a separate enum `whd_nan_events_t`: `WLC_E_NAN_EVENT_START` 1, `_JOIN` 2, `_ROLE` 3, `_SCAN_COMPLETE` 4, `_DISCOVERY_RESULT` 5, `_REPLIED` 6, `_TERMINATED` 7, `_RECEIVE` 8. No .c file in whd references them — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:376-381`
- whd — values 9..24 appear in one .c file only, as name strings in `whd_reason_to_string`, which has no caller in the whd tree — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:425` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:493-508`
- whd — values 1..8 of `whd_nan_events_t` overlap the roam reason codes in `whd_event_reason_t`; the header notes that reason values overlap and the event type must be checked — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:279-286` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:272-273`
- whd — declares `whd_nan_scan_handler` as extern; no definition or caller exists in the whd tree — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:441`
- embassy, c-driver — both leave this bit set in the mask they send, so they request the event and then ignore it (inferred from the mask code; no driver shows the event arriving) — `embassy/control.rs:307` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051`

### 101 BEACON_FRAME_RX
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `BEACON_FRAME_RX` = 101 `embassy/events.rs:202` / whd `WLC_E_BEACON_FRAME_RX` = 101, in the internal header only, not found in the public header `whd/inc/whd_events.h` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:133` / linux: not found — the event list goes from 75 to 92 to 127 with no value 101 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:95-97` / c-driver: not found — the `CYW43_EV_*` list has 17 defines, highest 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` / pico-sdk: not found — its `cybt_shared_bus*` files define no event codes `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus.c:1-421` `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus_driver.c:1-705` `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus_driver.h:1-81`. No spelling differences between embassy and whd. |
| Mask | embassy: default mask. Init sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 24 mask bytes of 255 (0xFF) and clears only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM; bit 101 (byte 12, bit 5) stays set `embassy/control.rs:307` `embassy/control.rs:311-317` `embassy/control.rs:319`. embassy also has a host-side mask that gates publishing; the only `enable()` calls are for SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so the driver itself never enables 101 there `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask. `cyw43_ll_wifi_on` sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 19 mask bytes of 255 (0xff) and clears only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71; bit 101 (byte 12, bit 5) stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2053-2057` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2060`. linux: not enabled. The mask starts zeroed and a bit is set only for a code with a registered handler, plus `BRCMF_E_IF` which is always set; linux has no enum value for 101, so no handler can be registered for it `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-450` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:456`. whd: not enabled by whd itself. The mask starts zeroed and bits are set only for events in a registered handler list; `WLC_E_BEACON_FRAME_RX` is in none of whd's own lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-416` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable any raw event number through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`; no caller exists in these sources `whd/src/whd_events.c:443` `whd/src/whd_events.c:510-512`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; the event is dropped in embassy at `embassy/runner.rs:1316`, in c-driver at `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312`, in linux at `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`, in whd at `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no record for this event's data found in embassy, c-driver, linux or whd. embassy maps it to `Payload::None` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`; c-driver parses event data only for ESCAN_RESULT `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`; linux returns before any handler sees the data `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`; whd passes data only to a registered handler `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` |

- embassy — no handler. The event type is cast to u8 and decoded, debug-logged, falls through the link-state match, and is not published because the driver never enables its host-side mask bit; the default payload arm is `Payload::None` — reads no field specific to this event (the generic path reads event_type, status, flags, reason, auth_type) — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`
- c-driver — no handler. `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for event type 101. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` set it is printed as the bare number, because the name table has 89 entries (0..88) — the trace print shows flags, event_type, status, reason, interface; nothing else is read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:303` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`
- linux — no handler. `brcmf_fweh_process_event` returns for a code with no registered handler. Code 101 is below the per-vendor code count (cyw 197, bca 212, wcc 213), so it passes the range check and is dropped at the handler check — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- whd — no handler. Both dispatch paths (CDC/BDC and msgbuf) call a handler only when the event type is in that handler's registered list; `WLC_E_BEACON_FRAME_RX` is in none — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865`
- whd — the name appears elsewhere only as a name string in `whd_event_to_string` and as a case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that falls into the default arm, which asserts "Received event which was not registered"; a source comment says the labels are there so that gcc's pedantic checks do not complain — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:318` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1571` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1614` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1340` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477`
- all drivers — status, reason and flags are not tested for this event; each driver drops it before any such test — `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912`
- whd, embassy — neither gives this event a description comment. In whd it shares a source line with `WLC_E_SERVICE_FOUND` = 102 and the trailing comment there describes 102 — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:133` `embassy/events.rs:202`
- linux — not the same event as linux `BRCMF_E_BEACON_RX` = 15 — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:41`
- embassy, c-driver — both leave this bit set in the mask they send, so they request the event and then ignore it (inferred from the mask code; no driver shows the event arriving) — `embassy/control.rs:307` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051`

- embassy — no handler. The event type is cast to u8 and decoded, debug-logged, falls through the link-state match, and is not published because the driver never enables its host-side mask bit; the default payload arm is `Payload::None` — reads no field specific to this event (the generic path reads event_type, status, flags, reason, auth_type) — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`
- c-driver — no handler. `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for event type 104. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` set it is printed as the bare number, because the name table has 89 entries (0..88) — the trace print shows flags, event_type, status, reason, interface; nothing else is read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:303` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`
- linux — no handler. `brcmf_fweh_process_event` returns for a code with no registered handler. Code 104 is below the per-vendor code count (cyw 197, bca 212, wcc 213), so it passes the range check and is dropped at the handler check — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- whd — no handler. Both dispatch paths (CDC/BDC and msgbuf) call a handler only when the event type is in that handler's registered list; `WLC_E_GAS_COMPLETE` is in none — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865`
- whd — the name appears elsewhere only as a name string in `whd_event_to_string` and as a case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that falls into the default arm, which asserts "Received event which was not registered"; a source comment says the labels are there so that gcc's pedantic checks do not complain — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:321` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1574` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1614` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1340` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477`
- all drivers — status, reason and flags are not tested for this event; each driver drops it before any such test — `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912`
- whd, embassy — source comment, same text in both: "GAS sessions all complete" — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:135` `embassy/events.rs:207`
- embassy, c-driver — both leave this bit set in the mask they send, so they request the event and then ignore it (inferred from the mask code; no driver shows the event arriving) — `embassy/control.rs:307` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051`

Init sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 24 mask bytes of 255 (0xFF) and clears only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM; bit 108 (byte 13, bit 4) stays set `embassy/control.rs:307` `embassy/control.rs:311-317` `embassy/control.rs:319`. embassy also has a host-side mask that gates publishing; the only `enable()` calls are for SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP and ESCAN_RESULT, so the driver itself never enables 108 there `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`. c-driver: default mask. `cyw43_ll_wifi_on` sets the named variable `bsscfg:event_msgs` with 19 mask bytes of 255 (0xff) and clears only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71; bit 108 (byte 13, bit 4) stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2053-2057` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2060`. linux: not enabled. The mask starts zeroed and a bit is set only for a code with a registered handler, plus `BRCMF_E_IF` which is always set; linux has no enum value for 108, so no handler can be registered for it `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-450` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:456`. whd: not enabled by whd itself. The mask starts zeroed and bits are set only for events in a registered handler list; `WLC_E_TXFAIL_THRESH` is in none of whd's own lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-416` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable any raw event number through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`; no caller exists in these sources `whd/src/whd_events.c:443` `whd/src/whd_events.c:510-512`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; the event is dropped in embassy at `embassy/runner.rs:1316`, in c-driver at `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312`, in linux at `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`, in whd at `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no record for this event's data found in embassy, c-driver, linux or whd. embassy maps it to `Payload::None` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`; c-driver parses event data only for ESCAN_RESULT `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`; linux returns before any handler sees the data `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498`; whd passes data only to a registered handler `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` |

- embassy — no handler. The event type is cast to u8 and decoded, debug-logged, falls through the link-state match, and is not published because the driver never enables its host-side mask bit; the default payload arm is `Payload::None` — reads no field specific to this event (the generic path reads event_type, status, flags, reason, auth_type) — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/runner.rs:1330`
- c-driver — no handler. `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for event type 108. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` set it is printed as the bare number, because the name table has 89 entries (0..88) — the trace print shows flags, event_type, status, reason, interface; nothing else is read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:303` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:744`
- linux — no handler. `brcmf_fweh_process_event` returns for a code with no registered handler. Code 108 is below the per-vendor code count (cyw 197, bca 212, wcc 213), so it passes the range check and is dropped at the handler check — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- whd — no handler. Both dispatch paths (CDC/BDC and msgbuf) call a handler only when the event type is in that handler's registered list; `WLC_E_TXFAIL_THRESH` is in none — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:865`
- whd — the name appears elsewhere only as a name string in `whd_event_to_string` and as a case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that falls into the default arm, which asserts "Received event which was not registered"; a source comment says the labels are there so that gcc's pedantic checks do not complain — `whd/src/whd_utils.c:325` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1578` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1614` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1340` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477`
- all drivers — status, reason and flags are not tested for this event; each driver drops it before any such test — `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:498` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:912`
- whd, embassy — source comment, same text in both: "Indication of MAC tx failures (exhaustion of 802.11 retries) exceeding threshold(s)" — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:139` `embassy/events.rs:215`
- whd, linux, c-driver — not the same event as `TXFAIL` = 20 (whd `WLC_E_TXFAIL`, linux `BRCMF_E_TXFAIL`); c-driver clears bit 20 from its mask and does not clear bit 108 — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:65` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:46` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2053`
- embassy, c-driver — both leave this bit set in the mask they send, so they request the event and then ignore it (inferred from the mask code; no driver shows the event arriving) — `embassy/control.rs:307` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051`

### 109 PROXD
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `PROXD` `embassy/events.rs:218` / linux not found: the event enum has no entry for this code and jumps from 92 to 127 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` / whd `WLC_E_PROXD` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:140`, name string `whd/src/whd_utils.c:326`, absent from the public `WLC_E_*` subset `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35-58` / c-driver not found: the `CYW43_EV_*` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:49-66`. Spelling after the prefix is identical in every driver that defines it. |
| Mask | embassy: left to a default mask. `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent as 24 bytes of 255 (0xFF) with only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG and ROAM cleared, so bit 109 stays set; not named explicitly `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. The driver-internal subscriber mask starts all-zero `embassy/events.rs:345-348`; embassy itself enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP (join) and ESCAN_RESULT (scan) in it `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`.<br>c-driver: left to a default mask. 19 mask bytes are set to 255 (0xff), bits 0..151, then only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared before `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent, so bit 109 stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. A narrower mask exists but is commented out `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294`.<br>whd: not enabled by the driver itself. whd first sets an all-zero 16-byte `event_msgs` mask `whd/src/whd_management.c:513-524`, then sets bits only for events in registered handler lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-418`. None of the internal lists contains this event `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable it by registering the code through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler` `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-483` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:230`.<br>linux: not enabled. `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` zeroes the mask and sets bits only for codes with a registered handler, plus IF `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456`; this code has no enum entry, so no handler. If the event arrives anyway linux drops it because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`. Before activation, linux reads the current `event_msgs`, sets only the IF bit and writes it back, so whatever value the read returned for bit 109 is written back unchanged `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`. Mask length is `num_event_codes` divided by 8, rounded up `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356`; the Cypress vendor module sets `num_event_codes` to 197, so bit 109 is inside the mask `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; each driver has only a generic path (see bullets) `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1579` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no driver defines a layout for it `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |

- embassy — generic path only: decodes to `Event::PROXD`, logs the message header and data bytes at debug level, takes the `_ => false` arm (no link-state change). It would publish the event with `Payload::None` only if the subscriber mask had it enabled, which embassy never does — reads `event_type`; `status` is decoded for every event, but no match arm names this event — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330`
- c-driver — no handler: every async event reaches `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`, whose if/else chain tests only ESCAN_RESULT, DISASSOC, DISASSOC_IND, PRUNE, SET_SSID, AUTH, DEAUTH_IND, LINK, PSK_SUP and ICV_ERROR, so this event is ignored. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` it is dumped by number; the name table has 89 slots — reads no field for this event (the parser byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason for every event; the dump prints flags, event_type, status, reason, interface) — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-309` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-291` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-749` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:806` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1278` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1315`
- whd — no real handler: the code appears only as a fall-through case of the join events handler, ending in an assert for an unregistered event. whd dispatches an event only to handlers whose registered list contains it, and `join_events` does not, so the case is not reached through whd's own registrations — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1579` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-925` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160`
- linux — no handler: the event enum has no entry for this code, so no handler can be registered for it; an event with this code is dropped because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Status/reason values tested: none. No driver tests status, reason or flags for this event `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Meaning, from the whd enum comment and the identical embassy doc comment: "Proximity Detection event" `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:140` `embassy/events.rs:217`
- Code 110 has no embassy variant (PROXD 109 is followed by AWDL_RX_PRB_RESP 111) `embassy/events.rs:218-220`. whd defines 110 twice, as `WLC_E_IBSS_COALESCE` and `WLC_E_MESH_PAIRED` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:141-142`.
- embassy converts the 32-bit `event_type` with `as u8` before the enum lookup, so only the low byte of the code selects this variant `embassy/runner.rs:1228`.

### 113 AWDL_WOWL_NULLPKT
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `AWDL_WOWL_NULLPKT` `embassy/events.rs:224` / linux not found: the event enum has no entry for this code and jumps from 92 to 127 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` / whd `WLC_E_AWDL_WOWL_NULLPKT` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:145`, name string `whd/src/whd_utils.c:330`, absent from the public `WLC_E_*` subset `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35-58` / c-driver not found: the `CYW43_EV_*` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:49-66`. Spelling after the prefix is identical in every driver that defines it. |
| Mask | embassy: left to a default mask. `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent as 24 bytes of 255 (0xFF) with only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG and ROAM cleared, so bit 113 stays set; not named explicitly `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. The driver-internal subscriber mask starts all-zero `embassy/events.rs:345-348`; embassy itself enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP (join) and ESCAN_RESULT (scan) in it `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`.<br>c-driver: left to a default mask. 19 mask bytes are set to 255 (0xff), bits 0..151, then only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared before `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent, so bit 113 stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. A narrower mask exists but is commented out `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294`.<br>whd: not enabled by the driver itself. whd first sets an all-zero 16-byte `event_msgs` mask `whd/src/whd_management.c:513-524`, then sets bits only for events in registered handler lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-418`. None of the internal lists contains this event `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable it by registering the code through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler` `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-483` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:230`.<br>linux: not enabled. `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` zeroes the mask and sets bits only for codes with a registered handler, plus IF `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456`; this code has no enum entry, so no handler. If the event arrives anyway linux drops it because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`. Before activation, linux reads the current `event_msgs`, sets only the IF bit and writes it back, so whatever value the read returned for bit 113 is written back unchanged `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`. Mask length is `num_event_codes` divided by 8, rounded up `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356`; the Cypress vendor module sets `num_event_codes` to 197, so bit 113 is inside the mask `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; each driver has only a generic path (see bullets) `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1583` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no driver defines a layout for it `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |

- embassy — generic path only: decodes to `Event::AWDL_WOWL_NULLPKT`, logs the message header and data bytes at debug level, takes the `_ => false` arm (no link-state change). It would publish the event with `Payload::None` only if the subscriber mask had it enabled, which embassy never does — reads `event_type`; `status` is decoded for every event, but no match arm names this event — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330`
- c-driver — no handler: every async event reaches `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`, whose if/else chain tests only ESCAN_RESULT, DISASSOC, DISASSOC_IND, PRUNE, SET_SSID, AUTH, DEAUTH_IND, LINK, PSK_SUP and ICV_ERROR, so this event is ignored. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` it is dumped by number; the name table has 89 slots — reads no field for this event (the parser byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason for every event; the dump prints flags, event_type, status, reason, interface) — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-309` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-291` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-749` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:806` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1278` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1315`
- whd — no real handler: the code appears only as a fall-through case of the join events handler, ending in an assert for an unregistered event. whd dispatches an event only to handlers whose registered list contains it, and `join_events` does not, so the case is not reached through whd's own registrations — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1583` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-925` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160`
- linux — no handler: the event enum has no entry for this code, so no handler can be registered for it; an event with this code is dropped because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Status/reason values tested: none. No driver tests status, reason or flags for this event `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Meaning, from the whd enum comment and the identical embassy doc comment: "AWDL Wowl nulls" `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:145` `embassy/events.rs:223`
- embassy converts the 32-bit `event_type` with `as u8` before the enum lookup, so only the low byte of the code selects this variant `embassy/runner.rs:1228`.

| Names | embassy `AWDL_AW_START` `embassy/events.rs:232` / linux not found: the event enum has no entry for this code and jumps from 92 to 127 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` / whd `WLC_E_AWDL_AW_START` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:149`, name string `whd/src/whd_utils.c:334`, absent from the public `WLC_E_*` subset `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35-58` / c-driver not found: the `CYW43_EV_*` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:49-66`. Spelling after the prefix is identical in every driver that defines it. |
| Mask | embassy: left to a default mask. `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent as 24 bytes of 255 (0xFF) with only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG and ROAM cleared, so bit 117 stays set; not named explicitly `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. The driver-internal subscriber mask starts all-zero `embassy/events.rs:345-348`; embassy itself enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP (join) and ESCAN_RESULT (scan) in it `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`.<br>c-driver: left to a default mask. 19 mask bytes are set to 255 (0xff), bits 0..151, then only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared before `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent, so bit 117 stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. A narrower mask exists but is commented out `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294`.<br>whd: not enabled by the driver itself. whd first sets an all-zero 16-byte `event_msgs` mask `whd/src/whd_management.c:513-524`, then sets bits only for events in registered handler lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-418`. None of the internal lists contains this event `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable it by registering the code through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler` `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-483` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:230`.<br>linux: not enabled. `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` zeroes the mask and sets bits only for codes with a registered handler, plus IF `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456`; this code has no enum entry, so no handler. If the event arrives anyway linux drops it because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`. Before activation, linux reads the current `event_msgs`, sets only the IF bit and writes it back, so whatever value the read returned for bit 117 is written back unchanged `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`. Mask length is `num_event_codes` divided by 8, rounded up `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356`; the Cypress vendor module sets `num_event_codes` to 197, so bit 117 is inside the mask `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; each driver has only a generic path (see bullets) `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1587` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no driver defines a layout for it `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |

- embassy — generic path only: decodes to `Event::AWDL_AW_START`, logs the message header and data bytes at debug level, takes the `_ => false` arm (no link-state change). It would publish the event with `Payload::None` only if the subscriber mask had it enabled, which embassy never does — reads `event_type`; `status` is decoded for every event, but no match arm names this event — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330`
- c-driver — no handler: every async event reaches `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`, whose if/else chain tests only ESCAN_RESULT, DISASSOC, DISASSOC_IND, PRUNE, SET_SSID, AUTH, DEAUTH_IND, LINK, PSK_SUP and ICV_ERROR, so this event is ignored. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` it is dumped by number; the name table has 89 slots — reads no field for this event (the parser byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason for every event; the dump prints flags, event_type, status, reason, interface) — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-309` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-291` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-749` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:806` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1278` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1315`
- whd — no real handler: the code appears only as a fall-through case of the join events handler, ending in an assert for an unregistered event. whd dispatches an event only to handlers whose registered list contains it, and `join_events` does not, so the case is not reached through whd's own registrations — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1587` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-925` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160`
- linux — no handler: the event enum has no entry for this code, so no handler can be registered for it; an event with this code is dropped because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Status/reason values tested: none. No driver tests status, reason or flags for this event `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Meaning, from the whd enum comment and the identical embassy doc comment: "AWDL AW Start" `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:149` `embassy/events.rs:231`
- embassy converts the 32-bit `event_type` with `as u8` before the enum lookup, so only the low byte of the code selects this variant `embassy/runner.rs:1228`.

| Names | embassy `CSA_START_IND` `embassy/events.rs:238` / linux not found: the event enum has no entry for this code and jumps from 92 to 127 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` / whd `WLC_E_CSA_START_IND` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:152`, name string `whd/src/whd_utils.c:338`, absent from the public `WLC_E_*` subset `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35-58` / c-driver not found: the `CYW43_EV_*` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:49-66`. Spelling after the prefix is identical in every driver that defines it. |
| Mask | embassy: left to a default mask. `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent as 24 bytes of 255 (0xFF) with only RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG and ROAM cleared, so bit 121 stays set; not named explicitly `embassy/control.rs:305-319`. The driver-internal subscriber mask starts all-zero `embassy/events.rs:345-348`; embassy itself enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP (join) and ESCAN_RESULT (scan) in it `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`.<br>c-driver: left to a default mask. 19 mask bytes are set to 255 (0xff), bits 0..151, then only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared before `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent, so bit 121 stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. A narrower mask exists but is commented out `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294`.<br>whd: not enabled by the driver itself. whd first sets an all-zero 16-byte `event_msgs` mask `whd/src/whd_management.c:513-524`, then sets bits only for events in registered handler lists `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-418`. None of the internal lists contains this event `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. An application can enable it by registering the code through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler` `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-483` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:230`.<br>linux: not enabled. `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` zeroes the mask and sets bits only for codes with a registered handler, plus IF `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456`; this code has no enum entry, so no handler. If the event arrives anyway linux drops it because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`. Before activation, linux reads the current `event_msgs`, sets only the IF bit and writes it back, so whatever value the read returned for bit 121 is written back unchanged `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`. Mask length is `num_event_codes` divided by 8, rounded up `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356`; the Cypress vendor module sets `num_event_codes` to 197, so bit 121 is inside the mask `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73`. |
| Handled by | no driver in these sources handles it; each driver has only a generic path (see bullets) `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1591` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; no driver defines a layout for it `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499` |

- embassy — generic path only: decodes to `Event::CSA_START_IND`, logs the message header and data bytes at debug level, takes the `_ => false` arm (no link-state change). It would publish the event with `Payload::None` only if the subscriber mask had it enabled, which embassy never does — reads `event_type`; `status` is decoded for every event, but no match arm names this event — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330`
- c-driver — no handler: every async event reaches `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`, whose if/else chain tests only ESCAN_RESULT, DISASSOC, DISASSOC_IND, PRUNE, SET_SSID, AUTH, DEAUTH_IND, LINK, PSK_SUP and ICV_ERROR, so this event is ignored. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` it is dumped by number; the name table has 89 slots — reads no field for this event (the parser byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason for every event; the dump prints flags, event_type, status, reason, interface) — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-309` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-291` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-749` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:806` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1278` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1315`
- whd — no real handler: the code appears only as a fall-through case of the join events handler, ending in an assert for an unregistered event. whd dispatches an event only to handlers whose registered list contains it, and `join_events` does not, so the case is not reached through whd's own registrations — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1591` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-925` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160`
- linux — no handler: the event enum has no entry for this code, so no handler can be registered for it; an event with this code is dropped because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Status/reason values tested: none. No driver tests status, reason or flags for this event `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Neither whd nor embassy carries a descriptive comment for this code; meaning beyond the name is not in these sources `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:152` `embassy/events.rs:238`
- Not the same event as CSA_COMPLETE_IND (80), which whd's join handler parses as `wl_chan_switch_t` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1455-1462` and c-driver names `CYW43_EV_CSA_COMPLETE_IND` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:64`.
- embassy converts the 32-bit `event_type` with `as u8` before the enum lookup, so only the low byte of the code selects this variant `embassy/runner.rs:1228`.

- Meaning, from the whd enum comment and the identical embassy doc comment: "CCA based channel quality report" `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:153` `embassy/events.rs:241`
- linux has CCA survey structures, but they belong to a named variable (iovar) query path, not to this event `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:117-123`. No driver defines an event data layout.
- embassy converts the 32-bit `event_type` with `as u8` before the enum lookup, so only the low byte of the code selects this variant `embassy/runner.rs:1228`.

### 125 BSSID

|---|---|
| Names | embassy `BSSID` `embassy/events.rs:244` / linux not found: the event enum has no entry for this code and jumps from 92 to 127 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` / whd `WLC_E_BSSID` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:154`, name string `whd/src/whd_utils.c:342`, absent from the public `WLC_E_*` subset `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35-58` / c-driver not found: the `CYW43_EV_*` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:49-66`. The driver-internal subscriber mask starts all-zero `embassy/events.rs:345-348`; embassy itself enables only SET_SSID, AUTH, PSK_SUP (join) and ESCAN_RESULT (scan) in it `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`.<br>c-driver: left to a default mask. 19 mask bytes are set to 255 (0xff), bits 0..151, then only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 are cleared before `bsscfg:event_msgs` is sent, so bit 125 stays set `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`. If the event arrives anyway linux drops it because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`. Before activation, linux reads the current `event_msgs`, sets only the IF bit and writes it back, so whatever value the read returned for bit 125 is written back unchanged `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`. - embassy — generic path only: decodes to `Event::BSSID`, logs the message header and data bytes at debug level, takes the `_ => false` arm (no link-state change). It would publish the event with `Payload::None` only if the subscriber mask had it enabled, which embassy never does — reads `event_type`; `status` is decoded for every event, but no match arm names this event — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330`
- c-driver — no handler: every async event reaches `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`, whose if/else chain tests only ESCAN_RESULT, DISASSOC, DISASSOC_IND, PRUNE, SET_SSID, AUTH, DEAUTH_IND, LINK, PSK_SUP and ICV_ERROR, so this event is ignored. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` it is dumped by number; the name table has 89 slots — reads no field for this event (the parser byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason for every event; the dump prints flags, event_type, status, reason, interface) — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-309` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-291` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-749` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:806` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1278` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1315`
- whd — no real handler: the code appears only as a fall-through case of the join events handler, ending in an assert for an unregistered event. whd dispatches an event only to handlers whose registered list contains it, and `join_events` does not, so the case is not reached through whd's own registrations — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1595` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-925` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160`
- linux — no handler: the event enum has no entry for this code, so no handler can be registered for it; an event with this code is dropped because `evt_handler[code]` is NULL — reads no field — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Status/reason values tested: none. No driver tests status, reason or flags for this event `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-499`
- Meaning, from the whd enum comment and the identical embassy doc comment: "to report change in BSSID while roaming" `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:154` `embassy/events.rs:243`
- No driver uses this event to track roaming: linux and c-driver define no identifier for it (`BRCMF_E_BSSID` and `CYW43_EV_BSSID` are not found in these sources) `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:26-97` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:49-66`, embassy does not use `Event::BSSID` outside its enum definition `embassy/events.rs:244`, and whd has `WLC_E_BSSID` only in the enum, the name-string table and the join-handler fall-through case `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:154` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:342` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1595`.
- embassy converts the 32-bit `event_type` with `as u8` before the enum lookup, so only the low byte of the code selects this variant `embassy/runner.rs:1228`.

- linux — `brcmf_fws_notify_bcmc_credit_support` sets `fws->bcmc_credit_check = true` under the fws lock. After that the dequeue worker no longer sends packets from the broadcast/multicast FIFO without credit: its loop runs while the FIFO has credit, or while `bcmc_credit_check` is false and the FIFO is `BRCMF_FWS_FIFO_BCMC` — reads no message field (not status, reason, flags, addr or data); receipt of the event alone is the signal — `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1629-1641` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:2233-2235` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:488` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:2195`
- linux registers this handler in `brcmf_fws_attach`, which the BCDC protocol layer calls `linux/brcmfmac/bcdc.c:430`. SDIO and USB select BCDC `linux/brcmfmac/bcmsdh.c:1088` `linux/brcmfmac/usb.c:1270`; PCIe selects msgbuf `linux/brcmfmac/pcie.c:2520`.
- embassy — generic path only: decodes to `Event::BCMC_CREDIT_SUPPORT`, logs the message header and data bytes at debug level, takes the `_ => false` arm (no link-state change). It would publish the event with `Payload::None` only if the subscriber mask had it enabled, which embassy never does — reads `event_type`; `status` is decoded for every event, but no match arm names this event — `embassy/runner.rs:1228-1235` `embassy/runner.rs:1293` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1330`
- c-driver — no handler: every async event reaches `cyw43_cb_process_async_event`, whose if/else chain tests only ESCAN_RESULT, DISASSOC, DISASSOC_IND, PRUNE, SET_SSID, AUTH, DEAUTH_IND, LINK, PSK_SUP and ICV_ERROR, so this event is ignored. With `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` it is dumped by number; the name table has 89 slots — reads no field for this event (the parser byte-swaps flags, event_type, status, reason for every event; the dump prints flags, event_type, status, reason, interface) — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-420` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:294-309` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-291` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:720-749` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:806` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1278` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:1315`
- whd — no real handler: the code appears only as a fall-through case of the join events handler, ending in an assert for an unregistered event. whd dispatches an event only to handlers whose registered list contains it, and `join_events` does not, so the case is not reached through whd's own registrations — reads no field — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1597` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1613-1615` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-925` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160`
- Status/reason values tested: none. The linux handler does not look at status, reason, flags or data `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:1629-1641`.
- Meaning, from the whd enum comment and the identical embassy doc comment: "credit check for BCMC supported" `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:156` `embassy/events.rs:247`
- Code 127 is the last bit of the 16-byte (128-bit) mask whd uses `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1932`. whd switches to the extended-mask named variable only for events above 127 `whd/src/whd_events.c:266-282`.
- embassy converts the 32-bit `event_type` with `as u8` before the enum lookup, so only the low byte of the code selects this variant `embassy/runner.rs:1228`.

- linux — no enum entry and no handler; the code is inside every vendor table's range (197, 212, 213) so it passes the range test and is dropped by the no-handler test — no field read — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`

- c-driver has a second `bsscfg:event_msgs` write (18 mask bytes) in the join path, but it is commented out; the init-time mask is the only one sent. The same holds for every event code. `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2291-2294` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`

| Mask | embassy: default mask, 24 bytes of 0xFF (bits 0-191) sent with `bsscfg:event_msgs`, this bit not cleared `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/structs.rs:437-440`; c-driver: default mask, 19 bytes of 0xff (bits 0-151) with only 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 cleared, sent with `bsscfg:event_msgs` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`; linux: not enabled, the mask is zeroed and a bit set only for codes with a registered handler `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-451`; preinit separately reads the `event_msgs` mask back and rewrites it with only the IF bit added, so linux does not clear this bit there `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`; whd: not enabled, the mask starts all-zero and only events in a registered handler's list get a bit, and no whd event list has this event `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-418` `whd/src/whd_management.c:516-524` |

- embassy — no specific handler; the generic path converts the code with `Event::from(event_type as u8)` and publishes the event to the queue only if a subscriber enabled it in the software mask; nothing in control.rs enables this one; payload is `Payload::None` — status, reason copied through, not tested — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1345` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`
- c-driver — no handler; `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` has no branch for this code; with `CYW43_TRACE_ASYNC_EV` it is printed as a bare number because the name table has no entry — no field read (flags, event_type, status, reason, interface printed only when tracing) — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:278-304`
- whd — no handler; the code is only a fall-through case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that ends in an assert for an unregistered event — no field read — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1600` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1612-1615`
- linux — no enum entry and no handler; the code is inside every vendor table's range (197, 212, 213) so it passes the range test and is dropped by the no-handler test — no field read — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`

No driver in these sources reads event 137, so whether it carries this header is unknown. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:110` `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:713-724` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1423-1424` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1964`

- whd — no handler; the code is only a fall-through case label in `whd_wifi_join_events_handler` that ends in an assert for an unregistered event — no field read — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1611` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1612-1615`
- linux — no enum entry and no handler; the code is inside every vendor table's range (197, 212, 213) so it passes the range test and is dropped by the no-handler test — no field read — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-498` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`

- whd `whd_event_to_string` returns the name string for it. `whd/src/whd_utils.c:203` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:193-194`
- Declared by whd, tested by no code: `WL_ULP_EVENT_VERSION` 1; `ulp_dongle_action` values `WL_ULP_DISABLE_CONSOLE` 1, `WL_ULP_UCODE_DOWNLOAD` 2, `WL_ULP_ENTRY` 3. `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:4142-4145`
- Codes 142-145 and 147-150 are not in the embassy enum. `embassy/events.rs:274-278`

### 187 EXT_AUTH_REQ
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `EXT_AUTH_REQ` `embassy/events.rs:280` / linux `BRCMF_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ`, an "abstract" id BIT(31)\|0, mapped to firmware code `BRCMF_CYW_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ` 187 only by the CYW vendor table `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:20-22` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:98` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:17` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:54` / whd `WLC_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ` (also in the public header) `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:173` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:55` / c-driver: not found, the `CYW43_EV_` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | linux: enabled, CYW vendor module only. The handler is registered at the firmware-code index through the event map, activate sets a bit for every index with a handler, and the cyw op sends the mask with the named variable (iovar) `event_msgs_ext` (ver 1, command `CYW_EVENTMSGS_SET_MASK` 3); mask length is DIV_ROUND_UP(197, 8) = 25 bytes `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356`; the plain `event_msgs` set is skipped when the vendor op returns 0 `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:361-370` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:396-410` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73-98`. whd: enabled only while an external-auth callback is registered; `whd_wifi_external_auth_request` registers `auth_events` = {187, 188} and `whd_wifi_stop_external_auth_request` deregisters; because the code is above 127 the mask is sent as `bsscfg:event_msgs_ext` (ver 1, command `EVENTMSGS_SET_MASK` 3), mask (188+8)/8 = 24 bytes after a 4-byte bsscfgidx and the `eventmsgs_ext_t` header `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:162-163` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3421-3438` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3453-3466` `whd/src/whd_events.c:266-284` `whd/src/whd_events.c:303-323` `whd/src/whd_events.c:410-418` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1045-1063`. embassy: default mask, 24 bytes of 0xFF (bits 0-191, so 187-189 are set), sent with the plain `bsscfg:event_msgs`, not `event_msgs_ext` `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/structs.rs:437-440`. c-driver: outside the mask; only 19 mask bytes (bits 0-151) are sent `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060` |
| Handled by | linux `brcmf_cyw_notify_ext_auth_req` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:239-272`; whd `whd_wifi_auth_events_handler` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3049-3076`; embassy and c-driver have no handler |
| Event data | 60 bytes, same field order in linux and whd. linux `struct brcmf_auth_req_status_le`, little-endian: `flags` le16 @0 (2); `peer_mac` u8[6] @2; `ssid_len` le32 @8 (4); `ssid` u8[32] @12; `pmkid` u8[16] @44 `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/fwil_types.h:53-59` `linux-headers/ieee80211.h:227` `linux-headers/ieee80211.h:2271`. whd `whd_auth_req_status_t`: uint16 `flags`, `whd_mac_t peer_mac`, uint32 `ssid_len`, `ssid[32]`, `pmkid[16]`; whd does not parse it `whd/inc/whd_types.h:1168-1175` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:61` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:93`. Offsets follow from natural alignment, no padding (computed, and compiled). linux reads only `ssid_len`, `ssid`, `peer_mac`; no driver reads the event's `flags` or `pmkid` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:262-264` |

- linux — rejects data shorter than the record, then asks the supplicant to start external SAE auth with `cfg80211_external_auth_request` (action `NL80211_EXTERNAL_AUTH_START`, key management `WLAN_AKM_SUITE_SAE`) using the SSID and BSSID from the data — reads datalen and data (`ssid_len` clamped to 32, `ssid`, `peer_mac`); status, reason, flags, addr not read — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:239-272`
- whd — passes the unparsed event data to the user's `auth_result_callback` with status `WHD_AUTH_EXT_REQ`, length given as sizeof(`whd_auth_req_status_t`) rather than datalen, plus a flag byte set to 1 when the saved scan record has `WHD_SCAN_RESULT_FLAG_SAE_H2E`; returns early if no callback is set — reads event_type only; data passed through unparsed — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3049-3076`
- embassy — no specific handler; the generic path publishes the event only if a subscriber enabled it in the software mask; nothing in control.rs does; payload is `Payload::None`; the code is converted with `as u8`, and 187 fits — status, reason copied through, not tested — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1345` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`
- c-driver — no handler: no branch for this code and no `CYW43_EV_` define — no field read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427`
- Status/reason values tested: none; no driver tests the event header's status, reason or flags. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:239-272` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3049-3076`
- The record's `flags` values are defined for the host reply (named variable `auth_status`), not tested on the event: linux `BRCMF_EXTAUTH_START` 1, `ABORT` 2, `FAIL` 3, `SUCCESS` 4; whd `WL_EXTAUTH_START` 1, `ABORT` 2, `FAIL` 3, `SUCCESS` 4. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/fwil_types.h:39-42` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:118-121`
- Reply paths: linux sets `flags` to SUCCESS or FAIL `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:196-231`; whd `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:5475-5506`.
- linux: the handler sees `e->event_code` as the abstract value, not 187. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:275` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:290`
- linux: preinit separately does a get/set of the plain named variable `event_msgs`, adding only the IF bit. `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-429`
- linux gates the surrounding external-SAE feature on firmware capability `sae_ext`; the handler itself is registered unconditionally in the cyw module. `linux/brcmfmac/feature.c:47` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:5149` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:7925` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:361-370`
- whd: before setting mask bits it clears only `WL_EVENTING_MASK_LEN` = 16 bytes, and the CDC buffer helper does not zero the data area, so bytes 16-23 of the 24-byte mask other than bits 187/188 are whatever the packet allocator returned (allocator is platform code, not in these sources). `whd/src/whd_events.c:409` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1932` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:349-366`
- whd: when its `WHD_FWCAP_SAE` capability flag is not set, whd sets `roam_off`, giving as reason that an EXT_AUTH_REQ raised during roaming would arrive after the host stopped the external auth request. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1869-1876`
- whd's enum comment is the same text for 187 and 188; embassy carries the same comments. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:173-174` `embassy/events.rs:279-282`
- whd `whd_event_to_string` has no case for it and returns "Unknown". `whd/src/whd_utils.c:351-353`

### 188 EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` `embassy/events.rs:282` / linux `BRCMF_E_EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX`, abstract id BIT(31)\|1, mapped to firmware code `BRCMF_CYW_E_EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` 188 only by the CYW vendor table `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:20-22` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:99` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:18` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:55` / whd `WLC_E_EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` (also in the public header) `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:174` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:56` / c-driver: not found, the `CYW43_EV_` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | linux: enabled, CYW vendor module only, through `event_msgs_ext` with a 25-byte mask (see 187) `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:361-370` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:396-410` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73-98`. whd: enabled only while an external-auth callback is registered; second entry of `auth_events`; sent with `bsscfg:event_msgs_ext`, 24-byte mask (see 187) `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:162-163` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3421-3438` `whd/src/whd_events.c:266-284` `whd/src/whd_events.c:303-323` `whd/src/whd_events.c:410-418`. embassy: default mask, 24 bytes of 0xFF (bits 0-191), plain `bsscfg:event_msgs` `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/structs.rs:437-440`. c-driver: outside the mask; only bits 0-151 are sent `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060` |
| Handled by | linux `brcmf_notify_auth_frame_rx` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:274-331`; whd `whd_wifi_auth_events_handler` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3077-3089`; embassy and c-driver have no handler |
| Event data | 16-byte header, then the received management frame (datalen - 16 bytes). linux `struct brcmf_rx_mgmt_data`, all big-endian: `version` be16 @0; `chanspec` be16 @2; `rssi` be32 @4; `mactime` be32 @8; `rate` be32 @12 `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:718-724`. whd `wl_rx_mgmt_data_t`: uint16 `version` @0; uint16 `channel` @2; int32 `rssi` @4; uint32 `mactime` @8; uint32 `rate` @12; whd names the second field `channel` where linux has `chanspec`, and reads no header field `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:76-83`. linux treats the frame bytes as starting with a full 802.11 management header: it skips offsetof(`struct ieee80211_mgmt`, `u`) bytes (24, computed from the fields before `u` with 6-byte addresses; linux's own `DOT11_MGMT_HDR_LEN` is also 24) and copies the rest as the auth body `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:318-320` `linux-headers/ieee80211.h:992-999` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.h:16` |

- linux — rebuilds an 802.11 auth management frame (frame control `IEEE80211_STYPE_AUTH`, da = own MAC, sa = event addr, bssid from `BRCMF_C_GET_BSSID`, body copied from the data after the 802.11 header offset) and passes it to `cfg80211_rx_mgmt` with `NL80211_RXMGMT_FLAG_EXTERNAL_AUTH` on the frequency decoded from the chanspec — reads datalen (must be at least the 16-byte header, and the frame part at least the management header length), addr, and data (`chanspec`, frame bytes); status, reason, flags not read — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:274-331`
- whd — skips the `wl_rx_mgmt_data_t` header and passes the frame bytes (length datalen - sizeof header) to the user's `auth_result_callback` with status `WHD_AUTH_EXT_FRAME_RX` — reads event_type, datalen, data (no header field) — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3077-3089`
- embassy — no specific handler; the generic path publishes the event only if a subscriber enabled it in the software mask; nothing in control.rs does; payload is `Payload::None` — status, reason copied through, not tested — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1345` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`
- c-driver — no handler: no branch for this code and no `CYW43_EV_` define — no field read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427`
- Status/reason values tested: none; neither handler tests status, reason or flags. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:274-331` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3077-3089`
- linux reads only `chanspec` from the header and picks the 2.4 GHz or 5 GHz band from the decoded value. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:306` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:322-325`
- whd does not check datalen >= sizeof(`wl_rx_mgmt_data_t`) before the unsigned subtraction; a shorter event would wrap the length. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:3083`
- linux computes the frame length (datalen - header size) before checking datalen; the check comes before any use. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:281` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:291`
- linux: the handler sees `e->event_code` as the abstract value, not 188. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:275` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:290`
- linux shares the header record with other events: its comment lists `WLC_E_PROBRESP_MSG`, `WLC_E_P2P_PROBREQ_MSG`, `WLC_E_ACTION_FRAME_RX`, and p2p.c uses it for action-frame and P2P probe-request events. `linux/brcmfmac/fwil_types.h:713-717` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1423-1424` `linux/brcmfmac/p2p.c:1964`
- The linux handler name has no `cyw` infix (`brcmf_notify_auth_frame_rx`) but is in cyw/core.c. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:275`
- whd's mask-clearing observation under 187 applies here too. `whd/src/whd_events.c:409` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1932`
- whd `whd_event_to_string` has no case for it and returns "Unknown". `whd/src/whd_utils.c:351-353`

### 189 MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS` `embassy/events.rs:284` / linux `BRCMF_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS`, abstract id BIT(31)\|2, mapped to firmware code `BRCMF_CYW_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXS` 189 (macro abbreviates to TXS) only by the CYW vendor table `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:20-22` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:100` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:19` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:56` / whd `WLC_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS` (also in the public header) `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:175` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:57` / c-driver: not found, the `CYW43_EV_` list ends at 88 `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | linux: enabled, CYW vendor module only, through `event_msgs_ext` with a 25-byte mask (see 187) `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:361-370` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:396-410` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-463` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73-98`. whd: not enabled by whd itself; it is not in `auth_events` or any other internal event list; it is in the public header, so an application could register it through `whd_wifi_set_event_handler` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:162-163` `whd/src/whd_events.c:410-418` `whd/src/whd_events.c:443` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:57`. embassy: default mask, 24 bytes of 0xFF (bits 0-191), plain `bsscfg:event_msgs` `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/structs.rs:437-440`. c-driver: outside the mask; only bits 0-151 are sent `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060` |
| Handled by | linux `brcmf_notify_mgmt_tx_status` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:333-359`; whd, embassy and c-driver have no handler |
| Event data | linux reads the first 4 bytes as a u32 packet id, dereferenced in host byte order with no conversion and no length check `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:338` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:345`. It compares it with `vif->mgmt_tx_id`, which is le32_to_cpu of the `packet_id` the host put in the named variable `mgmt_frame` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:157` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:167` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/fwil_types.h:82`. So `packet_id` u32 @0, little-endian as echoed (inferred from the round trip on a little-endian host; no driver states the byte order). Bytes after the first 4: no driver parses them |

- linux — handler shared with `MGMT_FRAME_OFFCHAN_DONE` (firmware code 190); returns without action unless a send is pending and the packet id in the data equals the stored one; for this event sets `BRCMF_MGMT_TX_ACK` or `BRCMF_MGMT_TX_NOACK` from status, then completes `vif->mgmt_tx`, which `brcmf_cyw_mgmt_tx` waits on — reads event_code, status, data (first 4 bytes); reason, flags, addr not read — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:333-359` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:367-370` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:178-186`
- whd — no handler; `whd_wifi_send_auth_frame` sets the named variable `mgmt_frame` and returns without registering or waiting for this event — no field read — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:4347-4374`
- embassy — no specific handler; the generic path publishes the event only if a subscriber enabled it in the software mask; nothing in control.rs does; payload is `Payload::None` — status, reason copied through, not tested — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316-1345` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828`
- c-driver — no handler: no branch for this code and no `CYW43_EV_` define — no field read — `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-427`
- Status values tested (linux): status == `BRCMF_E_STATUS_SUCCESS` (0) means acked; any other status means not acked. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:118` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:348-352`
- linux: the handler sees `e->event_code` as the abstract value, not 189. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:275` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:290`
- linux: the id matched is the cfg80211 cookie truncated to 32 bits. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:157`
- linux waits `MGMT_AUTH_FRAME_WAIT_TIME` = 4000 + 100 for the event; the value is passed as a jiffies count. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:23-24` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:178`
- whd uses a dwell time of 100 for the frame, and its `whd_auth_params_t` has a matching `packetId` field. `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:36` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:1195-1206`
- 189 is the last event in embassy's enum (`LAST` = 190). linux also defines firmware code 190 (`BRCMF_CYW_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXS_OC`), which the embassy and whd enums do not have. `embassy/events.rs:286` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:175-176`
- whd `whd_event_to_string` has no case for it and returns "Unknown". `whd/src/whd_utils.c:351-353`

## Events outside embassy's list

Event numbers and marker values that a driver defines but that are not in embassy's event list, what each driver does with a code it has no name for, and the event-mask length each driver sends. No source here establishes that any CYW43439 firmware sends or accepts any of these codes; every statement is about driver source.

### Coverage

- The main events chapter covers the 144 event numbers of embassy's `Event` enum other than `Unknown` and `LAST`, highest 189 (computed); this chapter covers every other number or marker value defined by a driver. `embassy/events.rs:14-287` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:25-101` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:42-185`
- Event code definitions compared: embassy's `Event` enum; linux's `BRCMF_FWEH_EVENT_ENUM_DEFLIST` and the vendor-module defines; whd's `whd_event_num_t` and its public subset; c-driver's `CYW43_EV_*` list. `embassy/events.rs:14-287` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:25-101` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:17-21` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:42-185` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35-58` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66`
- pico-sdk defines no Wi-Fi event codes (only an HCI packet type). `pico-sdk/cybt_shared_bus.c:47`
- embassy-pio, sdio-crate and linux-headers (bus and 802.11 headers): event code definitions not found in these sources.
- whd's alternate header set `include_helium` has no events header (the folder holds only `whd_wlioctl.h`), so there is no second whd event enum. `whd/src/include_helium/whd_wlioctl.h:194-223`
- The c-driver event name table uses only `CYW43_EV_` names and adds no number. `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276-292`
- linux also has the spellings `BRCMF_E_PROBERESP_MSG` and `BRCMF_E_P2P_PROBEREQ_MSG` for codes covered in the main events chapter; spelling only, no new number. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:91-92`
- Numbers below 203 named by no driver: 129, 142-145, 147-150, 152-156, 158-186, 191, 192, 194, 196, 197, 201 (computed from the definitions cited). `embassy/events.rs:14-287` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:25-101` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:17-21` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:42-185`

| Driver | Definitions outside the main events chapter | Citations |
|---|---|---|
| embassy | `Unknown` = 255 (0xFF), `LAST` = 190 | `embassy/events.rs:15-16` `embassy/events.rs:285-286` |
| linux (generic header) | 92; abstract values 0x80000000-0x80000003 | `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:96` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:98-101` |
| linux (vendor modules) | 190 as a firmware code (cyw only); table sizes 197 (cyw), 212 (bca), 213 (wcc) | `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20-21` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14` |
| whd (internal enum) | `WLC_E_NONE`, 92 (two names), 110 (two names), 157, 193, 195, 198, 199, 200, 202, `WLC_E_LAST` = 203 | `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:44` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:123-124` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:141-142` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:172-184` |
| whd (public header) | `WLC_E_NONE`, 157, 195 | `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:54` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:58` |
| c-driver | none; all 17 `CYW43_EV_*` values are in the main events chapter | `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |

- Firmware event codes outside the main events chapter: 92, 110, 157, 190, 193, 195, 198, 199, 200, 202. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:96` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:123-124` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:141-142` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:172-183`
- 190 exists as a firmware code only in linux's cyw module; embassy uses the same number for its `LAST` marker. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20` `embassy/events.rs:285-286`

### What each driver does with a code it does not name

These paths apply to every event in this chapter; the per-event bullets refer back to them.

- embassy — a number that is not an `Event` variant converts to the default variant `Unknown` = 255, after `event_type` is truncated to u8. `embassy/util.rs:41-46` `embassy/events.rs:15-16` `embassy/runner.rs:1228`
- embassy — the runner calls `is_enabled(event_type)` on the host-side subscription mask for every received event; there is no return or filter for `Unknown` between the conversion and that call (the link-state match in between ends in `_ => false`). `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1237-1294` `embassy/runner.rs:1316`
- embassy — the host-side mask has 6 u32 words (192 bits) and `is_enabled` indexes word n / 32; `Unknown` (255) gives index 7. See `### 255 Unknown` for the consequence. `embassy/events.rs:286` `embassy/events.rs:338-343` `embassy/events.rs:367-373`
- c-driver — the receive path converts the header and passes every event on with no range check. `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:735-748`
- c-driver — `cyw43_cb_process_async_event` is an if/else chain on known codes, so an unknown code does nothing; the branch at line 340 is inside `#if 0` and is not compiled. `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-416` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:339-345`
- c-driver — the trace dump prints a code with no name-table entry as a decimal number (table of 89 entries). `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:299-304`
- whd — dispatch has no range check on the code: after byte-swapping the header it compares `event_type` and the interface index with each registered list entry, so an unregistered code is ignored. `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:843-848` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-922`
- whd — a second dispatcher for msgbuf has the same list-match logic and no range check. `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:861-887`
- whd — "whd does not enable code N" in the event entries means no event list inside whd contains it. `whd_wifi_set_event_handler` is a public entry point that takes any uint32_t code list from the application, and whd would set the bit for it. `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-483`
- linux — a mask bit is set only for a code with a registered handler, plus IF. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456`
- linux — a received event whose code is >= the vendor module's `num_event_codes` is dropped. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-496`

### Event codes

#### 92 TDLS_PEER_EVENT / MESH_DHCP_SUCCESS
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | linux `BRCMF_E_TDLS_PEER_EVENT` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:96` / whd `WLC_E_TDLS_PEER_EVENT` and `WLC_E_MESH_DHCP_SUCCESS`, two enumerators with the same value 92 `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:123` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:124` / embassy: not found (enum goes AUTH_REQ = 91 to SPEEDY_RECREATE_FAIL = 93) `embassy/events.rs:185-187` / c-driver: not found `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | linux: inside the handler table and mask of all three vendor modules (cyw, wcc, bca) `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-463` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-496` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:73` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:31` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:30`; bit set only when the handler is registered, which happens in `brcmf_cfg80211_attach` only when feature `BRCMF_FEAT_TDLS` is present (detected by a GET of the named variable (iovar) `tdls_enable`) and setting `tdls_enable` = 1 succeeds `linux/brcmfmac/feature.c:333` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8662-8669`; `brcmf_fweh_activate_events` is called right after `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8674`. whd: inside the 16-byte `bsscfg:event_msgs` mask (bits 0-127); no whd event list contains either name, so whd does not enable it `whd/src/whd_events.c:266-323` `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-419` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1932` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-166` `whd/src/whd_ap.c:70` `whd/src/COMPONENT_WLANSENSE/whd_wlansense_core.c:44-46`. embassy: bit 92 is sent as 1 by the 0xFF fill of the 24-byte mask, although embassy has no variant with this number `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/structs.rs:437-447`. c-driver: bit 92 is sent as 1 by the 0xff fill of the 19-byte mask, although the c-driver has no define for this number `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060` |
| Handled by | linux `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:5942-5961` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:312-315`. whd: not handled `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1562` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1612-1615`. embassy and c-driver: no handler `embassy/events.rs:14-287` `embassy/util.rs:41-46` `embassy/events.rs:15-16` `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/events.rs:338-343` `embassy/events.rs:367-373` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:735-748` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-416` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:339-345` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:299-304` |
| Event data | no driver parses the data; linux uses only the header fields reason and addr (peer MAC) `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:5942-5961` |

- linux — `brcmf_notify_tdls_peer_event` switches on reason: 0 PEER_DISCOVERED (debug print only), 1 PEER_CONNECTED calls `brcmf_proto_add_tdls_peer(drvr, ifidx, addr)`, 2 PEER_DISCONNECTED calls `brcmf_proto_delete_peer(drvr, ifidx, addr)`; always returns 0 — reads reason, addr — `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:5942-5961` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:160-162`
- linux — with the BCDC protocol (set by the SDIO and USB bus code) both proto callbacks are empty functions; with msgbuf (set by the PCIe bus code) they update flow rings. `linux/brcmfmac/bcdc.c:388-398` `linux/brcmfmac/bcdc.c:463-464` `linux/brcmfmac/msgbuf.c:862-875` `linux/brcmfmac/msgbuf.c:1613-1614` `linux/brcmfmac/bcmsdh.c:1088` `linux/brcmfmac/usb.c:1270` `linux/brcmfmac/pcie.c:2520`
- linux — dispatch special case: for this firmware code the worker passes interface `iflist[0]` to the handler, not `iflist[bsscfgidx]` as for every other event; bsscfgidx is still range-checked — reads the event code — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:312-315` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:280-284`
- linux — reason values: 0 = `BRCMF_E_REASON_TDLS_PEER_DISCOVERED`, 1 = `_CONNECTED`, 2 = `_DISCONNECTED`; other reason values fall through the switch with no action. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:160-162` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:5946-5957`
- linux — the debug name table includes TDLS_PEER_EVENT because it is generated from the same DEFLIST. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:48-56`
- whd — not handled: `WLC_E_TDLS_PEER_EVENT` is only a case label in the join handler's group of unexpected cases that falls to the default assert; that code is reached only for events in `join_events`, and 92 is not in `join_events` — reads no fields — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1562` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1612-1615` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1477` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:156-160`
- whd — gives 92 two meanings by name (TDLS peer event; DHCP handshake success on a mesh interface). Which one a given firmware means is not stated in these sources. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:123-124`
- whd — `whd_event_to_string` lists `WLC_E_TDLS_PEER_EVENT` with `CASE_RETURN`, which is defined as `break`, so the function returns the literal "Unknown". `whd/src/whd_utils.c:196-197` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:309` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:358`
- whd — an unregistered code is ignored by dispatch. `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:843-848` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-922`
- embassy — converts to `Unknown` and reaches the `is_enabled` call described under `### 255 Unknown`; for 92 the precondition "the bit is enabled in the mask embassy sends" holds. `embassy/events.rs:14-287` `embassy/util.rs:41-46` `embassy/events.rs:15-16` `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/events.rs:338-343` `embassy/events.rs:367-373` `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/structs.rs:437-447`
- c-driver — no define; unknown-code path (no action; printed by number in the trace dump). `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:735-748` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-416` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:339-345` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:299-304`
- all drivers — no driver reads status for this event; whd has no reason values for it. `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:5942-5961` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1562`
- all drivers — whether CYW43439 firmware sends 92 is not stated in these sources; the evidence is the driver definitions only. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:96` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:123-124`
