| Event data | whd `wl_twt_setup_cplt_t`, 48 bytes, natural alignment (size and offsets computed from the member list; not taken from a whd build): offset 0 version (u16), 2 length (u16), 4 dialog (u8), 5 three bytes of compiler padding, 8 status (i32), 12 desc (`wl_twt_sdesc_t`, 36 bytes) `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:218-224` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:195-216`. No other driver parses the data |

- whd — `desc` layout, offsets from the start of desc: 0 setup_cmd (u8), 1 flow_flags (u8), 2 flow_id (u8), 3 wake_type (u8), 4 wake_time_h (u32), 8 wake_time_l (u32), 12 wake_dur (u32), 16 wake_int (u32), 20 btwt_persistence (u32), 24 wake_int_max (u32), 28 duty_cycle_min (u8), 29 pad (u8), 30 bid (u8), 31 channel (u8), 32 negotiation_type (u8), 33 frame_recomm (u8), 34 li (u16). `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:195-216`
- whd — record sizes and offsets (compiled: a re-typed copy of the definitions built with gcc on x86-64, not a whd build): sizeof 48 and 36; dialog 4, status 8, desc 12, negotiation_type 32. `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:195-224`
- whd — `whd_wifi_itwt_events_handler` casts the data to `wl_twt_setup_cplt_t`. If desc.negotiation_type is not 0 (`WHD_TWT_PARAM_NEGO_TYPE_ITWT`) it logs an unsupported negotiation type and sets session_state INACTIVE — reads event_type; data: desc.negotiation_type — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:4426-4476` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:1307`
- whd — otherwise by desc.setup_cmd: ACCEPT (4) sets session_state SETUP_COMPLETE, stores flow_id, dialog token, target wake time ((wake_time_h << 32) OR wake_time_l), wake_dur, wake_int and three booleans from flow_flags (IMPLICIT bit 1, TRIGGER bit 3, UNANNOUNCED bit 2 inverted), calls the user callback with `WHD_TWT_EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETE` and releases `twt_event_semaphore` — reads data: dialog, desc.setup_cmd, desc.flow_id, desc.flow_flags, desc.wake_time_h, desc.wake_time_l, desc.wake_dur, desc.wake_int — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:4426-4476`
- whd — setup_cmd REQUEST, SUGGEST, DEMAND, GROUPING, ALTERNATE, DICTATE (0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6) set SETUP_IN_PROGRESS with an error print; REJECT (7) or anything else sets NOT_COMPLETED. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:4426-4476`
- whd — converts the four 32-bit fields it reads with `dtoh32`; version, length and the payload status (i32 at offset 8) are not read by the handler, so success is inferred from setup_cmd only. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:4452-4454` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:4426-4476`
- whd — header status and reason are not read. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:4426-4476`
- whd — setup_cmd values: `TWT_SETUP_CMD_REQUEST` 0, SUGGEST 1, DEMAND 2, GROUPING 3, ACCEPT 4, ALTERNATE 5, DICTATE 6, REJECT 7. `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:171-178`
- whd — flow_flags values: `WL_TWT_FLOW_FLAG_IMPLICIT` 1<<1, UNANNOUNCED 1<<2, TRIGGER 1<<3. `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:227-229`
- whd — negotiation_type 0 = individual TWT. `whd/inc/whd_types.h:1307`
- whd — `WLC_E_TWT_SETUP` has no case in `whd_event_to_string`, so that function returns "Unknown" through default. `whd/src/whd_utils.c:351-353`
- whd — `include_helium/whd_wlioctl.h` carries a second copy of `wl_twt_sdesc_t` and `wl_twt_setup_cplt_t` with the same members in the same order. `whd/src/include_helium/whd_wlioctl.h:194-223`
- whd — an unregistered code is ignored by dispatch. `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:843-848` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-922`
- embassy — converts to `Unknown` and reaches the `is_enabled` call described under `### 255 Unknown`; bit 157 is inside embassy's 24-byte all-ones mask. `embassy/events.rs:14-287` `embassy/util.rs:41-46` `embassy/events.rs:15-16` `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/events.rs:338-343` `embassy/events.rs:367-373` `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/structs.rs:437-447`
- c-driver — no define; unknown-code path. `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:735-748` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-416` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:339-345` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:299-304`
- whd — TWT (target wake time; the 802.11ax attribution is recalled, not in these sources) is a whd feature; whether CYW43439 firmware implements it is not stated in these sources. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:172`

|---|---|
| Names | linux `BRCMF_CYW_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXS_OC` = 190, a firmware code defined in the cyw vendor module only `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20` / linux driver-side abstract name `BRCMF_E_MGMT_FRAME_OFFCHAN_DONE` = BIT(31) OR 3 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:101` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:57-60` / embassy: 190 is the variant `LAST`, not an event ("highest val + 1 for range checking"), used as the bit count of the host-side mask `embassy/events.rs:285-286` `embassy/events.rs:343` / whd: not found (enum goes MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS = 189 to DLTRO = 193) `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:175-176` / c-driver: not found `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |

- linux — `brcmf_notify_mgmt_tx_status`, the same function as for MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS (firmware code 189). Returns 0 without action unless `BRCMF_MGMT_TX_SEND_FRAME` is set on the vif and the u32 at the start of the data equals `vif->mgmt_tx_id` — reads data: packet id — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:333-359`
- linux — then, because `e->event_code` is not `BRCMF_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS`, it sets `BRCMF_MGMT_TX_OFF_CHAN_COMPLETED` and calls `complete(&vif->mgmt_tx)` — reads event_code, already translated to the abstract value — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:333-359` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:275` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:290` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.h:150-159`
- linux — the waiter is `brcmf_cyw_mgmt_tx` (auth frames sent with the named variable `mgmt_frame`), which after the wait tests only `BRCMF_MGMT_TX_ACK`. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:167-186`
- linux — the packet id compared is `vif->mgmt_tx_id`, taken from the cfg80211 cookie that was sent as `mf_params->packet_id = cpu_to_le32(cookie)`. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:157` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:167`
- linux — both 189 and 190 complete the same single completion object, so whichever arrives first with a matching packet id ends the wait. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:178` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:357`
- linux — status is read only in the 189 branch (SUCCESS gives ACK, else NOACK); for 190 neither status nor reason is used. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:348-352`
- linux — "off-channel done" is a reading of the linux names (TXS_OC, OFFCHAN_DONE, `BRCMF_MGMT_TX_OFF_CHAN_COMPLETED`); no driver documents the firmware semantics (inferred). `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:101` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.h:150-159`
- embassy — 190 is not `Unknown`: `Event::from(190)` yields the variant `LAST`. `embassy/util.rs:41-46` `embassy/events.rs:286`
- embassy — `is_enabled(LAST)` is word 5 bit 30, in range, and no code enables `LAST`, so a received 190 would be dropped at the `is_enabled` test without a panic. `embassy/events.rs:367-373` `embassy/control.rs:464` `embassy/control.rs:828` `embassy/runner.rs:1316`
- whd — its `auth_events` list holds only 187 and 188; 189 (`WLC_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS`) is named by whd but appears in no whd event list or handler; 190 has no whd name. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:162-163` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:175` `whd/inc/whd_events.h:57`
- c-driver — no define; unknown-code path. `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:735-748` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:312-416` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:339-345` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:276` `c-driver/cyw43_ctrl.c:299-304`
- all drivers — whether CYW43439 firmware sends 190 is not stated in these sources; the evidence is the linux cyw define only. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20`

- whd — `whd_wifi_icmp_echo_req_events_handler`: if no user callback is set it logs an error and returns — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:7146-7178`
- whd — if event_type is `WLC_E_ICMP_ECHO_REQ` and the payload version equals `WL_ICMP_ECHO_REQ_EVENT_VER` (1), it copies reason, echo_req_cnt, ip_ver and the peer address (4 bytes when ip_ver == `WHD_IPV4` = 1, else 16 bytes) into a `whd_icmp_echo_req_event_data_t` and calls the user callback; a payload with another version is ignored silently — reads event_type; data: version, reason, echo_req_cnt, ip_ver, u.ipv4.addr / u.ipv6.addr — `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:7146-7178`
- whd — reads version and echo_req_cnt with no byte-order conversion. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:7160` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:7163`
- whd — record size and offsets (compiled: a re-typed copy built with gcc on x86-64, not a whd build): sizeof 28; ip_ver 4, reason 5, echo_req_cnt 8, u 12. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:532-543`
- whd — header status and reason are not read. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:7146-7178`
- whd — in-payload reason (u8 at offset 5): `WLC_E_REASON_ICMP_ECHO_REQ_SUCCESS` 0, `_TIMEOUT` 1, `_DISASSOC` 2, cast to the public enum `IcmpEchoReq_event_reason_e`. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:525-527` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:7162` `whd/inc/whd_types.h:1588-1591`
- whd — ip_ver values: `WHD_IPV4` = 1, `WHD_IPV6` = 2. `whd/inc/whd_types.h:537-538`
- whd — gates the whole ICMP echo-request offload feature on the firmware capability string "icmp " (with a trailing space in whd's table). Whether CYW43439 firmware reports that capability is not stated in these sources. `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:6917` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:7187` `whd/src/whd_chip.c:93`
- whd — 202 is the highest event code defined by any driver here; `WLC_E_LAST` follows it with value 203. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:183-184`
- whd — not in `whd_event_to_string`; default returns "Unknown". `whd/src/whd_utils.c:351-353`
- linux — outside the cyw table, inside the wcc and bca tables, no name or handler. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14`
- embassy and c-driver — past both masks; not named. `embassy/control.rs:305-308` `embassy/structs.rs:437-440` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`

### Marker and pseudo values

Values that drivers define alongside the event codes but that are not firmware events, in the same entry layout.

#### 255 Unknown
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | embassy `Event::Unknown` = 255 (0xFF), the `#[default]` variant `embassy/events.rs:15-16` `embassy/util.rs:41-46` / linux, whd, c-driver: not found `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:25-101` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:42-185` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | embassy: not a firmware event; 255 is outside the 24-byte firmware mask (bits 0-191) and outside the 192-bit host-side mask `embassy/control.rs:305-308` `embassy/events.rs:343` |
| Handled by | embassy `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/events.rs:338-343` `embassy/events.rs:367-373` |
| Event data | not applicable; not an event `embassy/events.rs:15-16` |

- embassy — every received event number that is not an enum variant (after `as u8` truncation) becomes `Unknown` — reads event_type (low 8 bits) — `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `embassy/util.rs:41-46`
- embassy — the runner then calls `is_enabled(Unknown)`, which indexes word 255 / 32 = 7 of a 6-word array (`WORD_COUNT` = 190.div_ceil(32) = 6). `embassy/runner.rs:1316` `embassy/events.rs:338-343` `embassy/events.rs:367-373`
- embassy — that index is a Rust bounds-check panic for any received event whose number is not an enum variant (inferred from source; not observed in a running embassy). `embassy/events.rs:367-373`
- embassy — a standalone copy of the array length and index arithmetic (computed; not an embassy build) gives: `is_enabled(255)` panics with "index out of bounds: the len is 6 but the index is 7"; `is_enabled(190)` and `is_enabled(191)` return false. `embassy/events.rs:338-373`
- embassy — no filter on the event number exists between the header checks ending at line 1226 and the `is_enabled` call at line 1316. `embassy/runner.rs:1226` `embassy/runner.rs:1316`
- embassy — numbers that embassy maps to `Unknown` and whose bit is set in the 24-byte all-ones mask embassy sends: 92, 110, 157 (named by other drivers), plus every number in 0-191 named by no driver: 129, 142-145, 147-150, 152-156, 158-186, 191. `embassy/events.rs:272-286` `embassy/control.rs:305-319`
- embassy — 190 maps to the variant `LAST`, not `Unknown`. `embassy/events.rs:286`
- embassy — because event_type is truncated to u8 first, a wire code 0x100 + n would be read as n; the highest event code defined by any driver here is 202 and the largest linux table size is 213, both below 256. `embassy/runner.rs:1228` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:183` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- embassy — the same 0xFF default exists for `EStatus`; no use of it as an array index was seen (unverified). `embassy/consts.rs:256`
- embassy — `EventMask::unset` (firmware mask) indexes `events[evt / 8]` in a 24-byte array; it is only called with named variants <= 137. `embassy/structs.rs:443-447` `embassy/control.rs:311-317`

|---|---|

- embassy — `Event::LAST` = 190 sizes only the host-side subscription mask; the mask sent to the firmware is a fixed 24 bytes — `embassy/events.rs:285-286` `embassy/events.rs:343` `embassy/structs.rs:439`
- linux — cyw `BRCMF_CYW_E_LAST` = 197 sizes the handler table, mask length and the receive range check — `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:69-73` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495`
- whd — `WLC_E_LAST` = 203 sizes nothing; used only as a switch label. Mask length comes from `WL_EVENTING_MASK_LEN` or the highest registered code — `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:183-184` `whd/src/whd_utils.c:351` `whd/src/whd_wifi_api.c:1612` `whd/src/whd_events.c:287-304`
- linux — bca `BRCMF_BCA_E_LAST` = 212 sizes the handler table, mask length and the receive range check — `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495`
- linux — wcc `BRCMF_WCC_E_LAST` = 213 sizes the handler table, mask length and the receive range check — `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495`
- all drivers — the five markers disagree: embassy 190, linux cyw 197, whd 203, linux bca 212, linux wcc 213. `embassy/events.rs:285-286` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:183-184` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14`
- whd — 203 is the value in both WLANSENSE configurations because 202 is assigned explicitly (inferred from C enum rules). `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:178-184`
- linux — the SDIO device table assigns vendor modules per id: BROADCOM-vendor 43430 to CYW, BROADCOM-vendor 43439 to WCC, CYPRESS-vendor 43439 to CYW. Which table size linux uses for a "43439" therefore depends on the SDIO vendor id the chip reports. `linux/brcmfmac/bcmsdh.c:992` `linux/brcmfmac/bcmsdh.c:993` `linux/brcmfmac/bcmsdh.c:1004` `linux/brcmfmac/bcmsdh.c:973-977`

| Event data | not applicable; not an event `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35` |

- whd — marks the end of the event array list. `whd/inc/whd_events.h:35`
- whd — a list may hold at most `WHD_MAX_EVENT_SUBSCRIPTION` = 33 entries including the terminator. `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:447` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:518` `whd/src/whd_events.c:61-74`
- whd — the handler table `whd_event_list` has `WHD_EVENT_HANDLER_LIST_SIZE` = 5 entries, each holding one event list with its handler; registering a handler when no entry is free returns `WHD_OUT_OF_EVENT_HANDLER_SPACE`. `whd/src/include/whd_cdc_bdc.h:38` `whd/src/include/whd_cdc_bdc.h:103` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:515-522` `whd/src/whd_events.c:118-147`
- whd — the per-interface array `event_reg_list[WHD_EVENT_ENTRY_MAX]`, which stores the handler-table index of each registered kind, is a different array: `whd_event_entry_t` has 7 kinds (8 with `COMPONENT_WLANSENSE`; counted from the enum), more than the 5 handler-table entries. `whd/src/include/whd_int.h:86` `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:451-464`
- whd — `whd_event_to_string` returns the string "WLC_E_NONE" for it. `whd/src/whd_utils.c:214`

#### 2147483648-2147483651 linux abstract event codes
| | |
|---|---|
| Names | linux `BRCMF_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ` = BIT(31) OR 0, `BRCMF_E_EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` = BIT(31) OR 1, `BRCMF_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS` = BIT(31) OR 2, `BRCMF_E_MGMT_FRAME_OFFCHAN_DONE` = BIT(31) OR 3 `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:20-22` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:98-101` / whd has no abstract layer: `WLC_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ` 187, `WLC_E_EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` 188, `WLC_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS` 189 are plain firmware codes, no name for 190 `whd/src/include/whd_events_int.h:173-175` / embassy `EXT_AUTH_REQ` 187, `EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` 188, `MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS` 189 as plain codes, 190 is `LAST` `embassy/events.rs:280-286` / c-driver: not found `c-driver/cyw43_ll.h:50-66` |
| Mask | linux: an abstract code is never a mask bit or table index itself; it is replaced by the firmware code before any bit is set `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:193-211` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:396-413` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:438-452` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:65-99` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:361-371` |
| Handled by | linux `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:213-230` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:272-317` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:480-519` |
| Event data | not applicable (numbering scheme, never on the wire). Payloads of 187-189 are in the main events chapter; 190 is under `### 190 MGMT_FRAME_TXS_OC` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:98-101` |

- linux — `BRCMF_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ` = 2147483648 (0x80000000) maps to cyw firmware code 187, `BRCMF_CYW_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ` — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:98` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:17` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:54`
- linux — `BRCMF_E_EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` = 2147483649 (0x80000001) maps to cyw firmware code 188, `BRCMF_CYW_E_EXT_AUTH_FRAME_RX` — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:99` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:18` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:55`
- linux — `BRCMF_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXSTATUS` = 2147483650 (0x80000002) maps to cyw firmware code 189, `BRCMF_CYW_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXS` — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:100` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:19` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:56`
- linux — `BRCMF_E_MGMT_FRAME_OFFCHAN_DONE` = 2147483651 (0x80000003) maps to cyw firmware code 190, `BRCMF_CYW_E_MGMT_FRAME_TXS_OC` — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:101` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:20` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:57-60`
- linux — registration: `brcmf_fweh_register` calls `brcmf_fweh_map_event_code`, which replaces a code found in the vendor `event_map` by its firmware code and otherwise passes the code through unchanged; the handler table and the mask are indexed by that firmware code. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:193-211` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:396-413` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:334`
- linux — only the cyw module supplies a map (4 items) and only it registers the four abstract codes. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:52-63` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:361-371`
- linux — cyw sends the mask with `event_msgs_ext` { ver 1, command SET_MASK 3, len 25, maxgetsize 0, mask[25] }. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:65-99` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/fwil_types.h:11-37`
- linux — wcc and bca set no `event_map`. With no map the pass-through value BIT(31) OR n would be used directly as the table index in `brcmf_fweh_register`, with no bounds check in that function. `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:23-34` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:22-33` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:402-409`
- linux — no call site that registers an abstract code outside the cyw module exists; all `brcmf_fweh_register` calls are at `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:3787` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:4232` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:4279` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:6993-7027` `linux/brcmfmac/cfg80211.c:8668` `linux/brcmfmac/core.c:1399` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:2379` `linux/brcmfmac/fwsignal.c:2385` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:363-369`
- linux — receive: `brcmf_fweh_process_event` reads the big-endian firmware code, drops it if >= `num_event_codes` or if it has no handler (except IF), and queues it with the firmware code — reads event_type — `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:480-519`
- linux — the worker translates firmware code to abstract code with `brcmf_fweh_map_fwevt_code` only for the debug name and for `emsg.event_code` handed to the handler; the IF and TDLS special cases and the handler lookup keep using the raw firmware code. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:213-230` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:275` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:290` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:307` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:312` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:316`
- linux — handlers therefore compare `e->event_code` against abstract names. `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:348`
- linux — stated purpose: the mapping is needed when a functionally identical event has a different number between vendors, and the higher-layer code must not collide with a firmware event. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:297-300`
- linux — `BRCMF_ABSTRACT_ENUM_DEF(_id, _val)` expands to `BRCMF_ENUM_DEF(_id, (BRCMF_ABSTRACT_EVENT_BIT OR _val))`, so the abstract names also appear in the DEBUG name table with their bit-31 values and are looked up by abstract value. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:21-22` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:48-56` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:63-70` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:275-277`
- linux — the map is searched linearly in both directions. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:204-209` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:223-228`
- linux — consequence for a cross-driver table: linux's generic header contains no 187, 188, 189 or 190; those numbers exist only in the cyw module, and the linux names `BRCMF_E_EXT_AUTH_REQ` etc. must not be read as numeric codes. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:98-101` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:17-20`

### Event-mask length per driver

"Can cover" means the bit position exists in the bytes the driver sends. It does not mean the driver sets the bit or that any firmware honours it.

#### Mask sent by each driver
| Driver | Named variable | Mask bytes | Bit range | How it is built | Citations |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| embassy | `bsscfg:event_msgs` | 24 | 0-191 | fixed struct `EventMask { iface: u32, events: [u8; 24] }`, 28 bytes with the interface word; filled with 0xFF, then seven `unset()` calls for six distinct events (PROBRESP_MSG is unset twice): RADIO, IF, PROBREQ_MSG, PROBREQ_MSG_RX, PROBRESP_MSG, ROAM | `embassy/structs.rs:434-447` `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `embassy/control.rs:315-316` |
| c-driver | `bsscfg:event_msgs` | 19 | 0-151 | literal sizes: 19 bytes set to 0xff, then bits 20, 40, 44, 54, 71 cleared; command length 18 + 4 + 19 (name with NUL, 4-byte interface, mask) | `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2050-2060` |
| whd, legacy path | `bsscfg:event_msgs` (and plain `event_msgs` at init, in a retry loop) | 16 | 0-127 | `WL_EVENTING_MASK_LEN` = 16; used when no registered code is above 127; buffer = 4-byte bsscfgidx + 16 mask bytes, mask zeroed, then one bit per registered event | `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1932` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1074` `whd/src/whd_events.c:287-300` `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-419` `whd/src/whd_management.c:512-524` `whd/src/include/whd_utils.h:38-39` |
| whd, extended path | `bsscfg:event_msgs_ext` | (max_event + 8) / 8 with integer division, held in a uint8_t; max_event = highest registered code | 0 to at least max_event; the u8 length limits it to 255 bytes | chosen as soon as any registered code is > 127; payload = 4-byte bsscfgidx, then `eventmsgs_ext_t` { ver = `EVENTMSGS_VER` 1, command = `EVENTMSGS_SET_MASK` 3, len = mask_len, maxgetsize = 0, mask[] } | `whd/src/whd_events.c:259-285` `whd/src/whd_events.c:302-323` `whd/src/whd_events.c:409` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1045-1063` `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1075` `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-517` |
| linux cyw | `event_msgs_ext` for activation; plain `event_msgs` for the preinit read-modify-write | 25 | bytes hold bits 0-199; valid codes 0-196 (`num_event_codes` 197) | `event_mask_len` = DIV_ROUND_UP(197, 8) = 25; mask zeroed, one bit per registered handler index plus IF; sent as 4-byte header + 25 bytes, no bsscfg prefix | `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:65-99` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:381` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:438-463` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-496` `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:414-433` `linux/brcmfmac/fwvid.h:73-81` |
| linux wcc | `event_msgs` | 27 | bytes hold bits 0-215; valid codes 0-212 (`num_event_codes` 213) | `event_mask_len` = DIV_ROUND_UP(213, 8) = 27; no `activate_events` hook, so the generic path sets `event_msgs` with the 27-byte mask, no bsscfg prefix | `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:23-39` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:459-463` |
| linux bca | `event_msgs` | 27 | bytes hold bits 0-215; valid codes 0-211 (`num_event_codes` 212) | `event_mask_len` = DIV_ROUND_UP(212, 8) = 27; same generic `event_msgs` path as wcc | `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:22-38` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:356` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:459-463` |

- embassy, c-driver, whd — Bit n is byte n / 8, bit n % 8 in embassy, c-driver and whd. `embassy/structs.rs:444-447` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2052` `whd/src/whd_events.c:416` `whd/src/include/whd_utils.h:38-39`
- linux — sets bits with `setbit`; the macro definition is not found in these sources, so the bit order inside a byte is (unverified) for linux. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:450`
- all drivers — the drivers disagree on the mask length they send for the same family of firmware: 16 (whd legacy), 18 (c-driver, commented out), 19 (c-driver), 24 (embassy), 25 (linux cyw), 27 (linux wcc and bca), variable (whd extended). `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1932` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2050-2060` `embassy/structs.rs:434-447` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21` `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:14` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:14` `whd/src/whd_events.c:302-323`
- all drivers — no source here states what mask length a CYW43439 firmware requires or accepts. linux's comment says only that `num_event_codes` is the number of events supported by firmware, which does a minimum length check on the mask. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.h:330-333`
- all drivers — policy differs: embassy and c-driver enable nearly everything by fill, so they enable codes they cannot name; linux and whd enable only codes with a registered handler. `embassy/control.rs:305-319` `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2050-2060` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:444-456` `whd/src/whd_events.c:409-419`
- linux — abstract codes (bit 31) are outside every mask by construction and are translated to 187-190 on cyw before any bit is set. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:402-409` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:52-63`

#### Per-driver notes

- embassy — a separate host-side subscription mask is sized from the enum: `WORD_COUNT` = (LAST as u32).div_ceil(32) = 6 words = 192 bits. It never reaches the firmware; it filters what the runner publishes. `Unknown` (255) indexes outside it; see `### 255 Unknown`. `embassy/events.rs:286` `embassy/events.rs:338-343` `embassy/runner.rs:1316`
- embassy — every bit 0-191 except the six cleared events is sent as 1, including numbers embassy has no name for. `embassy/control.rs:305-319`
- c-driver — the 4 interface bytes are not written in the lines cited, so whatever the buffer held before is sent; what earlier code left in those bytes is (unverified). `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2051-2060`
- c-driver — a commented-out join-time alternative uses 4 + 18 bytes, an 18-byte mask, bits 0-143; not compiled. `c-driver/cyw43_ll.c:2282-2294`
- whd — at init whd sets an all-zero 16-byte `event_msgs` (no bsscfg prefix), retried up to `MAX_POST_SET_COUNTRY_RETRY` times. `whd/src/whd_management.c:512-524`
- whd — extended-path length examples (computed): only EXT_AUTH (187, 188) registered gives 24 bytes; TWT (157, 195) gives 25; CSI (198-200) or ICMP (202) gives 26. `whd/src/whd_events.c:302-304`
- whd — extended path: after obtaining the mask pointer the caller zeroes only `WL_EVENTING_MASK_LEN` = 16 bytes of it; the alloc helper zeroes only sizeof(`eventmsgs_ext_t`) = 5 bytes (header plus first mask byte); `whd_cdc_get_iovar_buffer` zeroes only the name-alignment bytes. At these sites mask bytes 16 to len - 1 are not explicitly cleared before the registered bits are OR-ed in. Whether the host buffer arrives zeroed is not found in these sources (unverified). `whd/src/whd_events.c:409` `whd/src/whd_events.c:319` `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:354-366`
- whd — `eventmsgs_ext_t` is declared with mask[1], so its sizeof is 5, and the data length requested is sizeof(`eventmsgs_ext_t`) + mask_len + 4 = mask_len + 9: 4-byte bsscfgidx, 4-byte header, mask_len mask bytes and one further byte past the mask, while the len field says mask_len. `whd/src/include/whd_wlioctl.h:1056-1063` `whd/src/whd_events.c:306-307` `whd/src/whd_events.c:322`
- whd — the mask is built and sent at two sites with identical code: `whd_management_set_event_handler` and the public `whd_wifi_set_event_handler`; both zero only 16 bytes. `whd/src/whd_events.c:396-421` `whd/src/whd_events.c:492-517` `whd/src/whd_events.c:409` `whd/src/whd_events.c:505`
- whd — `whd_wifi_set_event_handler` is public and takes any uint32_t code list from the application, so an application could register 92, 110, 193 etc. and whd would set the bit. `whd/src/whd_events.c:443-483`
- whd — `whd_wifi_deregister_event_handler` only clears the local list entry and sends no mask; the firmware mask is rebuilt only at the next registration. `whd/src/whd_events.c:597-626`
- whd — two receive dispatchers with the same list-match logic and no range check on the code: CDC/BDC and msgbuf. The event entries cite only the first. `whd/src/whd_cdc_bdc.c:906-922` `whd/src/whd_msgbuf_txrx.c:861-887`
- linux — preinit, all vendors: GET `event_msgs` into the mask, set bit IF, SET `event_msgs`. `linux/brcmfmac/common.c:415-428`
- linux — cyw: because cyw supplies `activate_events`, the mask goes out as `event_msgs_ext` and the generic `event_msgs` set is skipped. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:459-460` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:65-99`
- linux — cyw: the receive side drops codes >= 197. `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:495-496` `linux/brcmfmac/cyw/core.c:21`
- linux — wcc and bca: no `activate_events` hook, so `brcmf_fwvid_activate_events` returns -EOPNOTSUPP and the generic path sets `event_msgs`. `linux/brcmfmac/wcc/core.c:23-39` `linux/brcmfmac/bca/core.c:22-38` `linux/brcmfmac/fweh.c:459-463` `linux/brcmfmac/fwvid.h:73-81`
